Invariant Detail Table

ID Invariant Statement Why It Matters Enforced In Violation Detection Recovery / Debug
I1 BigUint has no trailing zero limbs; limbs.last() != Some(&0) (unless empty) Length is proxy for magnitude size in Algorithm D, comparison, threshold dispatch; a trailing zero corrupts quotient-digit estimation and length-based comparison bn-uint::normalize() called at every operation boundary debug_assert!(self.is_canonical()) at public fn entry; fuzz: normalize idempotence normalize() is O(k) trailing-zero strip; idempotent; safe to call unconditionally
I2 Sign::NoSign iff magnitude is zero; zero is never Plus or Minus Non-zero sign on zero magnitude breaks subtraction dispatch (wrong branch chosen) and GCD termination (non-zero length for zero operand) bn-int::BigInt constructors; bn-rational constructors; every arithmetic result constructor debug_assert_eq!(self.sign == NoSign, self.mag.is_zero()) in BigInt and BigRational After any op that can zero the magnitude, re-set sign to NoSign; enforced in BigInt::from_magnitude()
I3 Carry/borrow never lost; add result length ≤ max(n,m)+1; mul result length == n+m (before normalize) Dropped carry silently corrupts all higher limbs; wrong length triggers out-of-bounds access in subsequent operations bn-add, bn-mul inner loops; every carry captured in u128 debug_assert_eq!(result.limbs.len(), n+m) post-multiply; differential: schoolbook(a,b).len() == n+m always Carry loss is a hard algorithmic bug; not recoverable at runtime; diagnose via carry-trace feature
I4 Karatsuba(a,b) == Schoolbook(a,b), Toom3(a,b) == Schoolbook(a,b), NTT(a,b) == Schoolbook(a,b) for all a, b All multiply algorithms must be interchangeable via dispatch; wrong result from any path corrupts all higher-level operations bn-mul, bn-fftmul dispatch Phase 14 differential fuzz: 10,000 random pairs per size class; all four algorithms compared byte-for-byte Divergence: disable failing algorithm via feature flag; schoolbook remains; diagnose interpolation/CRT step
I5 divrem(a,b) → (q,r): a == q*b + r, 0 <= r < b; b == 0 → DivByZero Division contract; GCD, modpow, base conversion all require this identity; violated contract → GCD infinite loop bn-div (Knuth D path; Newton path) proptest: ∀ a, b ≠ 0: a == q*b + r ∧ 0 ≤ r < b; fuzz b=0 → DivByZero Algorithm D: at most 2 add-back corrections; Newton: correction step ≤ 2 adjustments; both provably bounded
I6 Newton-reciprocal divrem == Knuth D divrem for all valid inputs Newton is an optimization, not an alternative spec; two implementations of the same contract must agree bn-div Newton path Differential fuzz: same (a,b) pairs to both paths; hex-compare output If divergence: disable Newton via feature flag, investigate reciprocal correction step; re-enable after fix
I7 Every modular result ∈ [0, m); Montgomery: from_mont(to_mont(x)) == x mod m Out-of-range result breaks Barrett/Montgomery composition chain; wrong REDC → wrong modpow → wrong RSA output bn-mod (Barrett, Montgomery, REDC) debug_assert!(result < self.modulus) after every mod op; roundtrip test for Montgomery transform Barrett: final subtract if result ≥ m (at most 2); Montgomery: verify n' computation; check R selection
I8 NTT multiply reproduces exact integer product; no rounding for all supported sizes Integer arithmetic is exact; any rounding invalidates the library's correctness claims and downstream crypto bn-fftmul Differential fuzz NTT vs. schoolbook across NTT size range; all prime-carry paths checked If wrong: check prime p ≡ 1 mod 2^k; CRT coefficients (precomputed mu); accumulation overflow in pointwise step
I9 parse(format(x, base), base) == x; format produces the minimal exact string Base conversion is the external I/O boundary; a wrong digit corrupts every printed number and every parsed input bn-convert (schoolbook + divide-and-conquer paths) Roundtrip proptest for base 2, 8, 10, 16, random b; special cases: 10^k, 2^k, powers-of-two near thresholds Diagnose: compare divide-and-conquer vs. schoolbook repeated-divmod; check chunk boundary alignment and leading-zero handling
I10 BigRational always: gcd(num, den) == 1; den > 0; sign on numerator only Non-reduced rational gives wrong equality, wrong hash; wrong sign on den breaks comparison; den == 0 is undefined bn-rational constructors and every arithmetic result Debug: assert_canonical() after every arithmetic op; production: den != 0 enforced at construction with panic Reduce by gcd(num, den) after every arithmetic op; canonical sign: move sign from den to num; check in post-op assert
I11 In-place aliasing correctness: op(&mut a, &a) == op(a.clone(), a.clone()) for add_assign/sub_assign/mul_assign and their LimbsMut kernels Self-referential calls (a += &a, a.square_in_place(), modpow's squaring step) are common, not exotic; a kernel that reads a limb after overwriting it silently corrupts its own input only in the aliased case bn-add::add_limbs_in_place/sub_assign (read-once, alias-safe by construction); bn-mul Karatsuba/Toom3 (alias-detect-and-clone fallback) Differential aliasing property test: op(&mut a, &a) vs. op(a.clone(), a.clone()) for add/sub/mul across all dispatch algorithms and size classes Route the aliased call through a clone-then-op fallback; read-once kernels (schoolbook add/sub) are proven alias-safe and need no clone
I12 Montgomery round-trip: exit(REDC(enter(x))) == x mod n for all x; no public API function returns a value still in Montgomery form The Montgomery domain is an internal representation trick (P4/P6); a leaked Montgomery-form value silently poisons every downstream comparison, arithmetic op, or serialization that assumes normal form, with no error signal bn-mod::Montgomery::{into_form, from_form, redc}; public modpow/mod_mul wrappers always call from_form before returning Round-trip property test: from_form(into_form(x)) == x mod n for random (x, m); cargo miri test -p bn-mod checks for undefined behavior in the REDC limb-shift/cast sequence Audit every public bn-mod return path for a missing from_form call; introduce a zero-cost MontgomeryForm newtype marker if leaks recur, forcing conversion at the type boundary

Invariant Enforcement Architecture

  [ public API ]
       |
       v
  [ pre-condition debug_assert! is_canonical() ]
       |
       v
  [ limb kernel (bn-add / bn-mul / bn-div / bn-mod) ]
       |
       v
  [ normalize() — strip trailing zero limbs ]
       |
       v
  [ post-condition debug_assert! result.is_canonical() ]
       |
       v
  [ caller ]

Profiles and what is active:

Profile debug_assert! normalize() DivByZero return Differential oracle Fuzz targets
debug yes yes yes no no
release no yes yes no no
test yes yes yes yes (--features differential) no
fuzz yes yes yes yes yes (cargo fuzz)

Fuzz targets (bn-fuzz crate, Phase 14):

Target Inputs Oracle Invariants Checked
fuzz_add two random limb vecs schoolbook add I1, I2, I3
fuzz_mul two random limb vecs schoolbook mul I1, I3, I4
fuzz_div dividend + divisor Knuth D reference I1, I3, I5, I6
fuzz_mod value + modulus Barrett reference I1, I7
fuzz_gcd two random BigInt Python/GMP oracle I1, I2
fuzz_convert random BigUint roundtrip parse I9
fuzz_rational four random limbs (n,d,n2,d2) BigInt arithmetic I10

Invariant Debugging Methodology by ID

  • I1/I2 (canonical form): check_invariants() on BigUint/BigInt; failure location pinpoints last non-normalizing op; add normalize() call at that boundary; check all paths through the failing crate that return a BigUint.
  • I3 (carry): Feature carry-trace logs every carry/borrow value; compare trace vs. schoolbook expected carry sequence per-limb; look for the first limb position where traces diverge.
  • I4/I6/I8 (algorithm agreement): cargo test --features differential runs 10,000 random pairs per size class {8, 32, 64, 256, 1024, 4096 limbs}; disagreement panics with hex of failing inputs for bisection; bisect by size class first, then by sub-algorithm (Karatsuba split vs. base case).
  • I5 (division contract): proptest shrinks counterexamples automatically; first failure printed as (a_hex, b_hex, q_hex, r_hex); inspect Algorithm D add-back step count for the failing input.
  • I7 (modular range): Criterion benchmark wraps each mod op with range check in debug; panic includes {modulus, operands, result} in hex for REDC step isolation; check n_prime precomputation if Montgomery roundtrip fails.
  • I9 (roundtrip): Fuzz target fuzz_convert generates random BigUint, formats in base 10/16/rand, re-parses, compares; failing input printed as limb vector + base for chunk-boundary alignment analysis; check leading zero handling at chunk split points.
  • I10 (rational canonical): assert_canonical() is O(n log n) via GCD; used in all test/debug profiles; disabled in release via #[cfg(debug_assertions)]; check GCD reduction call site in the arithmetic result constructor for the failing operation.

Cross-Invariant Dependencies

I1 (canonical magnitude)
  -> used by: I3 (length predicts carry), I4 (schoolbook length == n+m),
              I5 (Algorithm D uses length as loop bound),
              I7 (Montgomery modulus length determines R)

I2 (sign canonical)
  -> used by: I10 (rational sign), BigInt comparison, GCD termination

I5 (division contract)
  -> used by: I9 (base conversion uses repeated divrem),
              I10 (gcd uses divrem internally)

I4 + I6 + I8 (algorithm agreement)
  -> all three are instances of P1; violation in any one corrupts all higher operations

8. Domain Model

8.1 Core Value Taxonomy

Entity Canonical Form Invariant Zero Representation
BigUint Vec<Limb>, little-endian, no trailing zeros I1 empty Vec or vec![0] normalized away
BigInt (Sign, BigUint) I2: NoSign iff mag.is_zero() (NoSign, BigUint{limbs:[]})
BigRational (Sign, num: BigUint, den: BigUint) I10: gcd=1, den>0, sign on num (NoSign, 0, 1)
Limb u64 scalar never aliased with carry state 0u64
DoubleLimb u128 transient never stored, only in register/stack —

8.2 Limb Buffer Layout

 index:  0          1          2      ...  n-1
 value: least-significant limb  ...  most-significant limb
 bits:  [63:0]    [127:64]  [191:128]  ... [64n-1 : 64(n-1)]
  • Storage: Vec<u64> heap-allocated; capacity managed by Rust allocator.
  • Normalization: strip trailing (highest-index) limbs that are zero; zero value → limbs.len() == 0.
  • Bit length: (limbs.len() * 64) - limbs.last().leading_zeros() (edge: 0 → bit_len = 0).
  • Endianness boundary: only the in-memory Vec is little-endian; Display/parse produce big-endian strings (I9).

8.3 Arithmetic State Machines

8.3.1 Addition State (carry propagation)

State: { result_limbs: Vec<Limb>, carry: u64 }
Transition per limb pair (a_i, b_i):
  sum = (a_i as u128) + (b_i as u128) + (carry as u128)
  result_limbs.push(sum as u64)
  carry = (sum >> 64) as u64
Post: if carry != 0 → append carry limb; normalize (I1)

State table:

carry_in a_i b_i carry_out result_limb
0 0 0 0 0
0 MAX MAX 1 MAX-1
1 MAX 0 1 0
1 MAX MAX 1 MAX
  • Length after add: at most max(len_a, len_b) + 1.
  • I3: carry_out captured in u128; never dropped.

8.3.2 Subtraction State (borrow propagation, requires a >= b)

State: { result_limbs: Vec<Limb>, borrow: i64 }
Transition per limb pair (a_i, b_i):
  diff = (a_i as i128) - (b_i as i128) - (borrow as i128)
  result_limbs.push(diff as u64)
  borrow = if diff < 0 { 1 } else { 0 }
Post: borrow must be 0 (a >= b precondition); normalize (I1)
  • Panic/error if borrow != 0 at end (unsigned underflow — caller must ensure a >= b).
  • BigInt layer: dispatches to magnitude add or sub based on Sign comparison (P7).

8.3.3 Multiplication Dispatch State Machine

           limb_count(a) + limb_count(b)
                  |
         ┌────────▼────────┐
         │ <= SCHOOL_THRESH │──── Schoolbook
         └────────┬────────┘
                  │
         ┌────────▼────────┐
         │ <= KARA_THRESH   │──── Karatsuba
         └────────┬────────┘
                  │
         ┌────────▼────────┐
         │ <= TOOM_THRESH   │──── Toom-Cook-3
         └────────┬────────┘
                  │
                NTT (bn-fftmul)

MulAlgo enum encodes the path; thresholds are tunable constants (Phase 14 benchmarks confirm them).

MulAlgo Threshold (limbs, approx) Asymptotic Invariant
Schoolbook 0–32 O(n²) baseline oracle (I4)
Karatsuba 33–256 O(n^1.585) == Schoolbook result (I4)
Toom3 257–2048 O(n^1.465) == Schoolbook result (I4)
Ntt > 2048 O(n log n) integer-exact via CRT (I5, I8)

8.3.4 Division State Machine (Knuth Algorithm D)

Precondition: b != 0
Normalize: shift = b.leading_zeros_in_top_limb(); a' = a << shift; b' = b << shift
Loop over quotient digits j from high to low:
  q̂ = (a'[j+n]*BASE + a'[j+n-1]) / b'[n-1]   -- two-limb estimate
  Correct q̂ down (at most 2 corrections per step)
  Multiply-subtract: a' -= q̂ * b'  (shifted)
  If underflow: add-back b', q̂ -= 1
Unnormalize remainder: r = a' >> shift
Post: DivRem { quotient, remainder } satisfying I5

Error path: b == 0 → DivByZero error (typed, never UB per P3, I5).

8.3.5 Newton-Reciprocal Division State

Input: divisor d (n limbs)
Goal: reciprocal r ≈ floor(BASE^(2n) / d)
Iteration (quadratic convergence):
  r_{k+1} = 2*r_k - r_k^2 * d >> (2n * LIMB_BITS)
Termination: |error| < 1 (proven in O(log n) steps)
Quotient: q = (a * r) >> (n * LIMB_BITS), then small correction (+/- 1)
Invariant I6: result equals Algorithm D result
  • Reuses bn-mul for multiplication within the Newton loop (P4).
  • Amortized cost when dividing many values by the same divisor: compute r once, reuse.

8.4 Montgomery / Barrett Modular Arithmetic Model

8.4.1 Barrett Reduction

Context Barrett { modulus: m, mu: BigUint, k: usize }
  mu = floor(BASE^(2k) / m)   precomputed once
Reduce(x):
  q̂ = (x * mu) >> (2k * LIMB_BITS)
  r  = x - q̂ * m
  while r >= m: r -= m         -- at most 2 corrections
  Invariant I7: result in [0, m)

8.4.2 Montgomery REDC

Context Montgomery { modulus: m, n_prime: Limb, r2: BigUint }
  R = BASE^n where n = limb count of m
  n' = (-m^{-1}) mod BASE          -- precomputed once
  r2 = R^2 mod m                   -- precomputed once

MontMul(ā, b̄):           -- ā = a*R mod m, b̄ = b*R mod m
  t = ā * b̄              -- full product
  u = (t mod R) * n' mod R
  result = (t + u*m) / R
  if result >= m: result -= m
  Invariant I7: result = a*b*R^{-1} mod m

Transform in:   ā = a * r2 mod m   (one MontMul with r2)
Transform out:  a = REDC(ā, 1)
Roundtrip:      out(in(x)) == x mod m   (I7)

8.4.3 Modular Exponentiation (windowed)

Window w (default 4 bits):
  Precompute table: g^1, g^3, g^5, ..., g^(2^w - 1) in Montgomery form
  Scan exponent from MSB in w-bit windows
  For each window:
    square w times
    multiply by precomputed table entry if window != 0
Constant-time option (P9): use constant-time table lookup (no branch on secret bits)

8.5 GCD State Machines

8.5.1 Binary (Stein) GCD

Inputs: a, b (BigUint)
State: { a, b, shift }
  1. shift = min(trailing_zeros(a), trailing_zeros(b)); a >>= shift; b >>= shift
  2. while b != 0:
       if a < b: swap(a, b)
       a -= b
       a >>= trailing_zeros(a)
  3. result = a << shift
  • Each iteration: at least one operand halved → O(n²) bit operations total.

8.5.2 Lehmer GCD (for large operands)

Extract top ~64 bits of a, b as â, b̂
Run Euclidean steps on scalars â, b̂; accumulate 2x2 matrix M
Apply M to full-precision (a, b): (a', b') = M * (a, b)  -- one multi-precision step
Recurse until b small enough for binary GCD
  • O(n log²n log log n) for n-limb inputs.

8.5.3 Extended GCD

Bezout coefficients: gcd(a,b) = s*a + t*b
State: (old_r, r, old_s, s, old_t, t)
  while r != 0:
    q = old_r / r
    (old_r, r) = (r, old_r - q*r)
    (old_s, s) = (s, old_s - q*s)
    (old_t, t) = (t, old_t - q*t)
Output: gcd = old_r; coeff_a = old_s; coeff_b = old_t
Use: modular inverse of a mod m = s mod m (when gcd == 1)

8.6 NTT Model

NttPlan { primes: [p0, p1, p2, ...], roots: [ω0, ω1, ...], len: usize }
  Each p_i is a prime where p_i ≡ 1 (mod len) so that a primitive len-th root of unity exists.
  Primes chosen: product of all p_i > max possible product coefficient (guarantees no wrap).

Forward NTT over p_i:
  bit-reverse permutation → butterfly stages (Cooley-Tukey)
  each butterfly: (a, b) → (a + ω^k * b, a - ω^k * b) mod p_i

Pointwise multiply: for each frequency bin j: C_j = A_j * B_j mod p_i

Inverse NTT, then CRT recombination:
  For each output index j: recover c_j via CRT from (c_j mod p0, c_j mod p1, ...)
  c_j is the exact integer coefficient.
Invariant I8: no floating-point; all arithmetic in Z_{p_i}.

8.7 Base Conversion Model

8.7.1 Schoolbook (small numbers)

To base B from BigUint x:
  digits = []
  while x != 0:
    (x, r) = divrem(x, B)
    digits.push(r)
  reverse(digits)
Cost: O(n * d) where d = output digit count, n = limb count per divmod.

8.7.2 Divide-and-Conquer (large numbers)

Power = B^(2^k) where 2^k >= ceil(n/2)  (precomputed powers table)
Split: x = x_hi * Power + x_lo
  x_hi = x / Power;  x_lo = x mod Power
Recurse on x_hi and x_lo independently
Concatenate digit strings with zero-padding for x_hi part
Cost: O(M(n) * log n) where M(n) = multiply cost
Threshold: switch from schoolbook when output > ~1000 digits

8.8 Sign-Magnitude Arithmetic (BigInt Layer)

Operation Sign rule
(+a) + (+b) +add_mag(a,b)
(+a) + (-b) sign(a-b) * sub_mag(max,min)
(-a) + (-b) -add_mag(a,b)
(+a) * (+b) +mul_mag(a,b)
(+a) * (-b) -mul_mag(a,b)
(-a) * (-b) +mul_mag(a,b)
(+a) / (-b) result sign from I5 convention; doc'd
any * 0 NoSign
  • Two's-complement bitwise: AND/OR/XOR/NOT on BigInt extend sign bit conceptually to infinity before applying limb-wise operations; internally handled by sign-aware dispatch without allocating infinite buffers.
  • NOT(x) = -(x+1) for BigInt (Python semantics).

8.9 BigRational Model

Canonical invariants (I10):
  gcd(num, den) == 1
  den > 0
  sign carried on num; den always Plus
  zero → (NoSign, 0, 1)

Construction: reduce(n, d):
  g = gcd(|n|, |d|)
  num = |n| / g
  den = |d| / g
  sign = sign(n) * sign(d)

Arithmetic:
  a/b + c/d = (a*d + c*b) / (b*d) → reduce
  a/b * c/d = (a*c) / (b*d)       → reduce
  a/b / c/d = (a*d) / (b*c)       → reduce (c != 0)

Comparison:
  a/b < c/d  iff  a*d < c*b  (cross-multiply, compare with sign)

8.10 Worked Examples

8.10.1 Addition: a = 2^64 - 1, b = 1

a.limbs = [0xFFFFFFFF_FFFFFFFF]  (1 limb)
b.limbs = [0x0000000000000001]   (1 limb)
i=0: sum = (0xFFFF...FFFF as u128) + 1 = 0x1_0000_0000_0000_0000
  result_limb[0] = 0x0000000000000000; carry = 1
post-loop: carry=1 → append limb 1
result.limbs = [0, 1]  == 2^64  ✓

8.10.2 Schoolbook Multiply: a = [3], b = [5]

acc: u128 = 0
i=0, j=0: prod = 3 * 5 + acc[0] = 15; result[0] = 15; carry = 0
result.limbs = [15]; normalize → [15] ✓  (== 15 in base 2^64)

8.10.3 Failure Path: Division by Zero

Input: divrem(BigUint::from(42), BigUint::zero())
Path: bn-div entry check: b.is_zero() == true → return Err(DivByZero)
No UB; no panic; typed error (I5).

8.10.4 Malformed Parse Input

parse("12XF9", Base10)
  At char 'X': not in '0'..'9' → return Err(ParseError::InvalidDigit { pos: 2, ch: 'X' })
parse("", Base10) → Err(ParseError::EmptyString)
parse("-0", Base10) → normalize to (NoSign, 0) after parse (I2)

8.10.5 Montgomery Roundtrip Edge Case: a = 0

in(0): ā = 0 * r2 mod m = 0
REDC(0, 1): t = 0*1 = 0; u = 0; result = 0 / R = 0; 0 < m → 0
out(0) = 0 mod m = 0 ✓   (I7 holds for zero)

8.10.6 Normalization Edge Case: BigUint after subtraction

a = [5, 3]  (== 3*2^64 + 5)
b = [5, 3]
sub(a, b):
  i=0: diff = 5-5-0 = 0; borrow=0
  i=1: diff = 3-3-0 = 0; borrow=0
result.limbs = [0, 0] → normalize → [] (I1)
sign → NoSign (I2)

8.11 Edge Cases Catalogue

Scenario Expected behavior Invariant
Add zero to any identity; no limb added I1, I3
Subtract value from itself result = BigUint::zero(), limbs=[] I1, I2
Multiply by zero BigUint::zero() I1, I4
Multiply by 1 clone of other operand I4
Very asymmetric operands (1 limb * 10000 limbs) Schoolbook dispatch; correct I4, P4
Shift left by 0 identity —
Shift right by >= bit_len BigUint::zero() I1
Karatsuba with odd limb split handles non-power-of-two split correctly I4
NTT with operand whose coefficient exceeds single prime CRT recombines correctly I8
Rational den = 0 at construction panic or Err (document choice) I10
BigInt sign of (-1)*(-1) +1, NoSign only for zero I2
Extended GCD when gcd != 1 Bezout coefficients still valid for gcd —
parse("0000007", Base10) normalizes to 7, no leading zeros in output I1, I9
base conversion base=1 Err(InvalidBase) I9
modpow(base, 0, m) returns 1 mod m I7
modpow(0, 0, m) convention: 1 (document) I7
Newton reciprocal when d has 1 limb fast-path: hardware division I6

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust (stable toolchain) C, C++, Zig, Go Memory safety without GC; zero-cost abstractions; ownership enforces I3 (no silent carry drop); u128 native for DoubleLimb; cargo ecosystem C/C++ when per-microarchitecture hand-written assembly is required to reach GMP-class throughput; Go when GC-managed simplicity and fast compile outrank raw speed
ADR-02 Build system Cargo (workspace with [workspace]) Bazel, CMake, Meson Native Rust; crate-level incremental compilation; workspace unifies all bn-* crates under one Cargo.lock Bazel in a large polyglot monorepo with remote caching; CMake/Meson when embedding into an existing C/C++ build
ADR-03 Workspace structure 12 crates in single workspace Monorepo with independent manifests, single mega-crate Per-crate compile isolation; downstream can depend on bn-uint without bn-fftmul; compile-time boundary enforcement A single mega-crate when the library always ships as one unit and per-crate dependency slicing is never needed
ADR-04 Dependency policy num-traits for Zero/One/Num traits only; otherwise zero external dependencies in library crates num-bigint as substrate, rug/GMP bindings From-scratch educational goal; GMP bindings would bypass the implementation; num-traits is interface-only, no algorithm code rug/GMP bindings when production-grade speed is the only goal and the from-scratch mandate does not apply
ADR-05 Limb word width u64 (Limb); u128 for double-limb u32/u64 mixed (GMP style), u32 only x86-64 and AArch64 have 64-bit multiply; u128 in Rust is compiler-intrinsic, not a dependency; halving limb count halves loop iterations u32 limbs on 32-bit targets lacking a 64-bit multiplier or where u128 intrinsics are unavailable
ADR-06 Limb endianness Little-endian Vec<Limb> (index 0 = least significant) Big-endian, mixed Add/sub/mul naturally scan low-to-high; resize (push/pop) touches the high end (cheap); matches GMP mpn convention Big-endian when interoperating with a big-endian wire format where avoiding byte-swap on I/O outweighs arithmetic scan convenience
ADR-07 Serialization / wire format None in library crates (bn-core through bn-rational); bn-convert owns Display / from_str serde derive in library Serialization policy belongs to consumers; no mandatory serde dep avoids compile-time bloat in constrained environments serde in core when the dominant use is (de)serialization-heavy and every consumer already accepts the dependency
ADR-08 bn-cli I/O format Plain text: newline-separated decimal/hex; --base N flag JSON, binary packed Human-readable for a calc/bench tool; JSON adds serde_json dep unnecessarily JSON when the CLI feeds a structured tooling pipeline; binary packed for high-throughput operand transfer between processes
ADR-09 Error handling thiserror-derived typed errors (ParseError, DivByZero) returned as Result<T, E>; no panic on arithmetic anyhow everywhere, panic-on-error Library code must not panic on user input (I5); thiserror zero-cost, no runtime overhead; anyhow appropriate only in binaries (bn-cli) anyhow in an application binary where error kinds are never matched; panic-on-error only in a throwaway script
ADR-10 Multiplication dispatch Compile-time constants for thresholds; runtime branch in bn_mul::mul Trait objects / dynamic dispatch, proc-macro dispatch Branch is a single integer compare; trait objects add vtable indirection per call; constants tunable by benchmarks in Phase 14 Dynamic dispatch when algorithms must be pluggable at runtime by users (a research/experimentation harness comparing variants)
ADR-11 NTT prime selection Three hard-coded NTT-friendly primes (≡ 1 mod 2^k) with product > max coefficient Dynamic prime search at runtime Deterministic, reproducible; verified once offline; dynamic search adds startup cost and complexity Dynamic prime search when operand sizes exceed what the fixed prime product supports and the range must extend at runtime
ADR-12 Montgomery context caching Montgomery struct holds precomputed n_prime and r2; caller constructs once per modulus Recompute per operation Modular exponentiation calls REDC O(log e) times per exponentiation; amortized precomputation is mandatory (P6) Recompute-per-op when each modulus is used exactly once, making precomputation pure overhead
ADR-13 Constant-time modpow Optional feature flag ct-modpow; default is variable-time; ct path uses constant-time table lookup Always variable-time, always constant-time Variable-time is faster for non-crypto use; ct required for crypto consumers (P9); feature flag lets callers opt in without overhead Always-constant-time when the library is crypto-only and any variable-time path is a liability; always-variable-time when no secret ever flows through it
ADR-14 Concurrency / async None — library crates are fully synchronous; Send + Sync derived where Vec<u64> allows rayon parallelism in NTT, async API Correctness-first (P1); parallel NTT is a Phase 14 extension if benchmarks justify; library is not an async executor rayon when huge-operand multiply is wall-clock-bound on many cores; an async API when the bignum is wrapped behind a network service
ADR-15 Testing framework #[test] + proptest (property-based) + cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) quickcheck, custom harness proptest has richer shrinking; cargo-fuzz is de-facto Rust fuzzing standard; no bespoke framework maintenance quickcheck in a codebase already standardized on it; a custom harness when inputs need domain generation proptest cannot express
ADR-16 Differential / conformance testing Compare against num-bigint (Rust) and Python int via subprocess GMP via C FFI in test harness num-bigint is pure Rust, no C FFI in CI; Python subprocess is portable; GMP FFI adds C toolchain dependency and unsafety GMP FFI oracle when matching GMP's exact behaviour (not just correctness) is the goal and a C toolchain in CI is acceptable
ADR-17 Benchmarking criterion (statistical, HTML reports) iai, hyperfine, custom timing Criterion is the Rust standard; wall-clock statistical with noise reduction; needed to tune MulAlgo thresholds (Phase 14) iai (instruction-count) for noise-free CI regression detection on shared runners; hyperfine for whole-binary CLI timing
ADR-18 Logging / tracing None in library crates; tracing (with tracing-subscriber) in bn-cli only log crate, env_logger tracing is structured and composable; library crates must not emit to stderr/stdout (consumer decides); log is a lesser alternative The log facade when consumers are standardized on log and structured spans add no value
ADR-19 Metrics / observability None in library; bn-cli bench subcommand emits JSON timing to stdout Prometheus metrics, OpenTelemetry Library has no daemon; bench output is machine-parseable for CI regression detection without a telemetry stack Prometheus/OpenTelemetry when the bignum runs inside a long-lived service needing live metrics, not a one-shot CLI
ADR-20 CI/CD GitHub Actions: cargo test --workspace, cargo clippy, cargo fmt --check, cargo fuzz (short duration), criterion regression check CircleCI, Jenkins, bare Makefile GitHub Actions free for public repos; native Rust caching (sccache); fuzz in CI catches regressions before merge Self-hosted Jenkins for private repos needing custom hardware or air-gapped builds
ADR-21 Fuzz targets cargo-fuzz targets: fuzz_parse, fuzz_divmod, fuzz_modpow, fuzz_from_to_base Only unit tests, AFL Fuzz covers the parser (I9), division (I5, I6), modpow (I7), and conversion (I9) — highest-risk code surfaces Unit-only for a frozen, fully-covered API with no untrusted input; AFL for whole-binary, file-driven fuzzing
ADR-22 Packaging / distribution crates.io for each bn-* crate; [workspace.package] for unified version Single crate with feature flags, private registry Per-crate publishing lets consumers depend on bn-uint alone; feature flags would entangle compile graph A single feature-flagged crate when consumers always want the whole library and partial dependency is never a use case
ADR-23 MSRV (minimum supported Rust version) Rust 1.79 stable (for u128 carry ops, core::hint::black_box) nightly for intrinsics, oldest stable u128 stable since 1.26; black_box stable since 1.66; 1.79 gives carry_add / borrowing_sub on nightly-stabilized paths if desired Nightly when unstable carry intrinsics are needed for peak speed; oldest-stable when supporting very conservative downstreams
ADR-24 Licensing MIT OR Apache-2.0 (dual) GPL, MIT-only, proprietary Standard Rust crate dual-license; compatible with GMP-derived ideas (ideas, not code); permissive for downstream embedding GPL when copyleft reciprocity is a goal; MIT-only when Apache's explicit patent grant is undesirable for a particular downstream
ADR-25 Reproducible builds Cargo.lock committed; workspace pins all deps; CI builds use locked deps (--locked) Floating deps, no lockfile Ensures bit-identical artifacts across machines; prevents supply-chain surprises in dependencies Floating deps for a library meant to ride upstream fixes (applications pin the lockfile; libraries float)
ADR-26 Unsafe code policy #![forbid(unsafe_code)] in all library crates; unsafe permitted only in bn-cli for black_box benchmark helper Unsafe limb kernels for performance Correctness over micro-optimizations (P1); Rust's optimizer handles u128 arithmetic without unsafe; unsafe adds audit burden Unsafe limb kernels when a profiled hot loop needs SIMD intrinsics the safe optimizer won't emit and the audit cost is accepted
ADR-27 Feature flags bn-mod: ct-modpow for constant-time path; bn-fftmul: ntt-parallel (Phase 14 extension placeholder); no other flags Pervasive feature flags per algorithm Minimize conditional compilation complexity; two well-defined flags cover the known use-case split Pervasive per-algorithm flags when binary-size minimization for embedded targets dominates over build simplicity
ADR-28 Documentation cargo doc + #[doc] on all public items; examples in doc-tests; ADR decisions referenced in module-level docs Separate wiki, mdBook Doc-tests are compile-tested; no drift between docs and code; cargo doc --open gives immediate browsable API An mdBook/wiki when there is a large conceptual narrative (tutorials, theory) beyond the API reference
ADR-29 Containers Docker image for CI (rust:1.79-slim); no runtime container required for the library Alpine, distroless, none rust:slim avoids musl cross-compilation issues for criterion benchmarks; library ships as source, not container distroless/alpine for a minimal production runtime image when the bignum is deployed as a service rather than source
ADR-30 Algorithm cross-check strategy In #[test]: for each of Karatsuba/Toom3/NTT, generate random inputs, verify result == schoolbook (I4); Newton divrem == Knuth (I6); Montgomery roundtrip (I7) Runtime assertions, debug-only checks Tests run in CI; runtime assertions in hot path violate P4 (paid even in production); separation keeps kernels fast Debug-only runtime assertions when shipping a debug build to a consumer who wants in-production self-checks and accepts the slowdown

11. Core Schema and Core Types

11.1 bn-core types

Limb / DoubleLimb / SignedDoubleLimb

type Limb           = u64;
type DoubleLimb     = u128;   // limb*limb + carry; never stored
type SignedDoubleLimb = i128; // borrow capture for subtraction; never stored
Property Detail
Ownership Copy; no allocation
Mutation Immutable values; only as temporaries in kernels
Concurrency Send + Sync trivially (Copy)
Persistence Serialize as little-endian bytes (u64 LE)
Compatibility Alias stability guaranteed; changing word size is a semver break
Constants LIMB_BITS = 64; LIMB_MASK = u64::MAX; LIMB_BYTES = 8

Sign

enum Sign { Minus, NoSign, Plus }
Property Detail
Invariant NoSign iff magnitude is zero (I2)
Ownership Copy
Concurrency Send + Sync
Persistence Serialize as i8: -1 / 0 / +1
Derived PartialEq, Eq, Clone, Copy, Debug, Hash

ParseError / DivByZero

enum ParseError { EmptyString, InvalidDigit { pos: usize, ch: char }, InvalidBase(u32) }
struct DivByZero;
Property Detail
Ownership Clone; no heap unless message formatting
Concurrency Send + Sync
Trait impls std::error::Error; Display; From conversions into a library-wide Error

11.2 bn-uint types

BigUint

struct BigUint { limbs: Vec<Limb> }
Property Detail
Invariant No trailing zero limbs; zero == empty vec OR single zero limb normalized away (I1)
Ownership Owns its limb buffer; Clone = heap copy
Mutation normalize() — strips trailing zeros; called after every in-place operation
Concurrency Send + Sync; no interior mutability
Persistence Serialize as [u8] little-endian (8 bytes per limb, strip trailing zero bytes)
Compatibility limbs field is private; layout change permitted inside a major version
Canonical zero limbs.is_empty() or equivalently limbs == [0] after normalize is always []

Limbs<'a> / LimbsMut<'a>

struct Limbs<'a>    { inner: &'a [Limb] }
struct LimbsMut<'a> { inner: &'a mut [Limb] }
Property Detail
Purpose Borrowed slice views passed into in-place kernels; avoid exposing Vec internals
Ownership Borrowed; lifetime tied to owning BigUint
Mutation Limbs — immutable; LimbsMut — mutable in-place
Concurrency Limbs: Sync; LimbsMut: exclusive, not Sync while held

MulAlgo

enum MulAlgo { Schoolbook, Karatsuba, Toom3, Ntt }
Property Detail
Selection Dispatch by limb count; thresholds in bn-mul::dispatch; tunable via consts
Ownership Copy
Observability Returned by select_mul_algo(n_limbs: usize) -> MulAlgo; inspectable in tests

11.3 bn-div types

DivRem

struct DivRem { quotient: BigUint, remainder: BigUint }
Property Detail
Invariant a == quotient * b + remainder AND 0 <= remainder < b (I5)
Ownership Owns both fields; produced by divrem(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> Result<DivRem, DivByZero>
Mutation Immutable after construction
Persistence No direct serialization; serialize constituent BigUint fields

11.4 bn-mod types

Montgomery

struct Montgomery { modulus: BigUint, n_prime: BigUint, r2_mod_n: BigUint }
Property Detail
Invariant n_prime == -modulus^{-1} mod R; r2_mod_n == R^2 mod modulus; modulus must be odd (I7)
Construction Montgomery::new(modulus: BigUint) -> Result<Self, MontgomeryError>
Ownership Owns all fields; reuse context across many operations (P6)
Mutation Immutable after construction; no interior mutation
Concurrency Send + Sync; share across threads for parallel modpow
Persistence Serialize modulus only; recompute n_prime and r2 on deserialize

Barrett

struct Barrett { modulus: BigUint, mu: BigUint, k: usize }
Property Detail
Invariant mu == floor(b^{2k} / modulus); k == limb count of modulus
Construction Barrett::new(modulus: BigUint) -> Self
Ownership Owns all fields
Usage Reduce a value < modulus^2 in two multiplications; reuse context (P6)
Concurrency Send + Sync
Persistence Serialize modulus + k; recompute mu

11.5 bn-fftmul types

NttPlan

struct NttPlan { primes: Vec<u64>, roots: Vec<u64>, len: usize }
Property Detail
Invariant Each prime is NTT-friendly (prime ≡ 1 mod len); product of primes > max output value (I8)
Construction NttPlan::for_operands(a_limbs: usize, b_limbs: usize) -> NttPlan
Ownership Owns primes/roots vecs; reuse for same operand sizes
Persistence Not serialized; recomputed from operand sizes

11.6 bn-int types

BigInt

struct BigInt { sign: Sign, mag: BigUint }
Property Detail
Invariant sign == NoSign iff mag is zero (I2); zero has unique representation
Ownership Owns mag (heap); Clone = deep copy
Mutation set_sign_and_normalize() — enforce I2 after magnitude mutation
Concurrency Send + Sync
Persistence Serialize as (sign: i8, mag: [u8] little-endian)
Compatibility Field access private; construction via BigInt::from_parts(sign, mag)
Signed comparison Minus < NoSign < Plus; magnitudes compared within same sign

11.7 bn-convert types

Base

enum Base { Base10, Base16, BaseN(u32) }
Property Detail
Valid range BaseN(n): 2 <= n <= 36; else ParseError::InvalidBase
Ownership Copy
Usage Passed to format(x: &BigUint, base: Base) -> String and parse(s: &str, base: Base) -> Result<BigUint, ParseError>

11.8 bn-rational types

BigRational

struct BigRational { sign: Sign, num: BigUint, den: BigUint }
Property Detail
Invariant gcd(num, den) == 1; den != 0; sign in {Minus, NoSign, Plus} on numerator; den always Plus (I10)
Construction BigRational::new(num: BigInt, den: BigInt) -> Result<Self, DivByZero> — reduces by GCD internally
Ownership Owns sign, num, den
Mutation Every arithmetic result calls reduce() before returning
Concurrency Send + Sync
Persistence Serialize as (sign, num bytes, den bytes); validate invariant on deserialize

11.9 Type cross-reference and trait bounds summary

11.10 Normative API signatures (no bodies — Rule 10)

// bn-uint
impl BigUint {
    fn new(limbs: Vec<Limb>) -> Self;              // normalizes on construction
    fn normalize(&mut self);                       // strip trailing zero limbs; enforce I1
    fn bit_length(&self) -> usize;                 // floor(log2(self)) + 1; 0 for zero
    fn from_u64(v: u64) -> Self;
    fn from_u128(v: u128) -> Self;
    fn to_u64(&self) -> Option<u64>;               // None if > u64::MAX
}

// bn-add
fn add_limbs(dst: LimbsMut<'_>, a: Limbs<'_>, b: Limbs<'_>) -> Limb; // returns carry
fn sub_limbs(dst: LimbsMut<'_>, a: Limbs<'_>, b: Limbs<'_>) -> Limb; // requires a>=b; returns borrow
fn add_mul_limb(acc: LimbsMut<'_>, b: Limbs<'_>, c: Limb) -> Limb;   // acc += b*c; returns carry

// bn-mul
fn mul(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> BigUint;                          // dispatches via MulAlgo
fn select_mul_algo(n_limbs: usize) -> MulAlgo;
fn schoolbook_mul(a: Limbs<'_>, b: Limbs<'_>, out: LimbsMut<'_>);
fn karatsuba_mul(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> BigUint;
fn toom3_mul(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> BigUint;
// bn-fftmul
fn ntt_mul(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint, plan: &NttPlan) -> BigUint;

// bn-div
fn divrem(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> Result<DivRem, DivByZero>;
fn divrem_single(a: &BigUint, b: Limb) -> Result<(BigUint, Limb), DivByZero>;
fn newton_reciprocal(d: &BigUint, k: usize) -> BigUint;               // floor(b^k / d)

// bn-mod
fn barrett_reduce(x: &BigUint, ctx: &Barrett) -> BigUint;
fn montgomery_mul(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint, ctx: &Montgomery) -> BigUint;
fn modpow(base: &BigUint, exp: &BigUint, ctx: &Montgomery) -> BigUint;
fn modpow_ct(base: &BigUint, exp: &BigUint, ctx: &Montgomery) -> BigUint; // constant-time (P9)

// bn-gcd
fn gcd(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> BigUint;
fn lehmer_gcd(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> BigUint;
fn ext_gcd(a: &BigInt, b: &BigInt) -> (BigUint, BigInt, BigInt);       // (gcd, s, t): a*s + b*t = gcd
fn mod_inverse(a: &BigUint, m: &BigUint) -> Option<BigUint>;           // None if not invertible

// bn-convert
fn format(x: &BigUint, base: Base) -> String;
fn parse(s: &str, base: Base) -> Result<BigUint, ParseError>;

// bn-rational
impl BigRational {
    fn new(num: BigInt, den: BigInt) -> Result<Self, DivByZero>;
    fn reduce(&mut self);                          // enforce I10
    fn to_decimal(&self, scale: usize) -> String;  // scale decimal places, truncating
}

Phase 1 — core types + magnitude representation

1.1 Objectives

  • Define the entire shared vocabulary in bn-core: Limb, DoubleLimb, SignedDoubleLimb, Sign, ParseError, DivByZero.
  • Define BigUint in bn-uint as { limbs: Vec<Limb> } little-endian, canonical (no trailing zero limbs).
  • Implement normalization, comparison, and From/TryFrom conversions for primitive widths up to u128.
  • Establish Limbs<'a> / LimbsMut<'a> borrowed-slice views for in-place kernel use.
  • Constraint: no arithmetic beyond comparison and scalar conversion in this phase; all higher operations depend on this baseline.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

bn-core (src/lib.rs)

Symbol Definition Notes
Limb type Limb = u64 storage word; always little-endian
DoubleLimb type DoubleLimb = u128 captures limb×limb overflow
SignedDoubleLimb type SignedDoubleLimb = i128 borrow-capture for subtraction
Sign enum { Minus, NoSign, Plus } NoSign iff magnitude is zero
ParseError enum { InvalidDigit(u8), EmptyString, InvalidBase(u32) }
DivByZero struct DivByZero zero-size typed error
LIMB_BITS const = 64u32
LIMB_BYTES const = 8usize

bn-uint (src/lib.rs)

  • BigUint { limbs: Vec<Limb> } — pub(crate) field access only; external access via methods.
  • fn normalize(limbs: &mut Vec<Limb>) — truncates trailing zeros; sets limbs to empty for value 0.
  • fn is_normalized(limbs: &[Limb]) -> bool — asserts invariant I1; used in debug-mode assertions.
  • Limbs<'a> — struct Limbs<'a>(&'a [Limb]); LimbsMut<'a> — struct LimbsMut<'a>(&'a mut [Limb]).
  • Comparison (PartialOrd, Ord): compare length first; if equal, compare limbs high-to-low (index n-1 down to 0).
  • impl From<u64> for BigUint: single-limb vec, normalized.
  • impl From<u128> for BigUint: two limbs [lo, hi], normalized.
  • impl From<u32>, From<u8>, From<usize> delegating to From<u64>.
  • fn to_u64(&self) -> Option<u64>: Some iff limbs.len() <= 1.
  • fn to_u128(&self) -> Option<u128>: Some iff limbs.len() <= 2.
  • fn zero() -> BigUint: empty limbs, canonical.
  • fn is_zero(&self) -> bool: limbs.is_empty().
  • fn limb_count(&self) -> usize: limbs.len().
  • fn bit_len(&self) -> u64: (limbs.len() − 1) × 64 + top-limb significant bits (computed with u64::BITS - limb.leading_zeros()); 0 for zero.
  • All constructors call normalize; all mutating ops must re-normalize before returning.
  • Anchors: I1 (canonical magnitude — normalize()/is_normalized() are defined here and are the invariant's sole enforcement point); P2 (canonical representation); ADR-05 (u64 Limb / u128 DoubleLimb), ADR-06 (little-endian limb order).

1.3 Deliverable

  • bn-core crate compiles; all types exported.
  • bn-uint crate compiles; BigUint::zero(), From<u64/u128>, Ord, normalize, bit_len, to_u64, to_u128 all functional.
  • Unit tests and property tests pass (see 1.4).

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
normalize idempotency: normalize(&mut v); normalize(&mut v) == v 10 000 proptest cases
Ord is total order: reflexive, transitive, antisymmetric 10 000 proptest cases
From<u128> roundtrip: BigUint::from(x).to_u128() == Some(x) all u128 proptest values
Canonical zero: BigUint::from(0u64).limbs.is_empty() unit test
bit_len matches x.leading_zeros() for u64 1000 random cases
All debug assertions: is_normalized never panics on a returned value in-CI debug build

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected
fixtures/uint/normalize_vectors.json Pairs of (raw limb vec, canonical vec) normalized == canonical vec
fixtures/uint/compare_pairs.json Pairs with Less/Equal/Greater tag Ord result matches tag
fixtures/uint/from_u128.json u128 values + expected limb vectors limbs match; to_u128 roundtrips
fixtures/uint/bit_len.json BigUint hex + expected bit length exact match
fixtures/uint/malformed_trailing_zero.json Limb vecs with trailing zeros normalize removes them

1.6 Pitfalls

  • Trailing-zero normalization omitted after mutation: a caller extends limbs via push, forgets normalize; equality and hash diverge from value. Detection: run is_normalized on every BigUint returned from public API in debug mode.
  • bit_len off-by-one for exact powers of two: u64::BITS - leading_zeros() = 64 when limb = 0, but the top limb should never be zero after normalization; if normalization is skipped, bit_len overcounts by 64. Root cause: accepting an un-normalized BigUint. Recovery: assert normalization.
  • from(0u64) producing limbs = [0] instead of []: violates I1 and I2; affects comparison and zero-test. Fix: normalize in every From impl.
  • Endianness confusion: mistaking big-endian for little-endian when indexing limbs. Manifestation: comparisons and multi-limb arithmetic produce wrong answers. Use index 0 = least-significant throughout; document in every array access.
  • to_u64 returning None for canonical representations of small values: if a two-limb BigUint has limbs [x, 0], normalize should collapse it to one limb; failing to do so causes to_u64 to return None for a small value.

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • Vec<Limb> allocation per value is unavoidable; small-integer path (limbs.len() <= 2) should not heap-allocate in future phases — reserve a SmallVec upgrade path in struct layout notes.
  • normalize is O(trailing zeros found) — worst case O(n) scan from the end; in practice O(1) for non-degenerate inputs.
  • Clone of BigUint is a heap allocation; avoid unnecessary clones in kernel loops (use Limbs<'a> views instead).
  • Ord comparison terminates early on length mismatch: keep length check before limb scan.

1.8 Edge Cases

Input Expected behavior
from(0u64) limbs = [], is_zero() = true
from(u64::MAX) limbs = [u64::MAX]
from(u128::MAX) limbs = [u64::MAX, u64::MAX]
from(1u128 << 64) limbs = [0, 1]
Compare two canonical zeros Equal
bit_len on zero 0
to_u64 on 2-limb value with hi=0 should normalize to 1 limb, return Some

1.9 Failure Analysis

  • Violation of I1: any non-normalized BigUint escaping a public boundary corrupts comparison, hash, and any algorithm that checks length. Blast radius: incorrect equality in hash maps; overcounting in multiplications; wrong result from bit_len. Detection: debug_assert!(is_normalized(&self.limbs)) in every public method entry.
  • Silent overflow in bit_len: unchecked limb index access on empty limbs. Recovery: special-case limbs.is_empty() -> 0.
  • Postmortem signal: failing property tests on normalize idempotent or total order invariants in CI.

1.10 Operational Notes

  • Enable debug_assert!(is_normalized) in all non-release builds; it is the primary correctness signal for Phase 1.
  • Expose a BigUint::from_limbs_unchecked(limbs: Vec<Limb>) -> BigUint for test fixtures only, clearly documented as bypassing normalization; gate it behind #[cfg(test)].
  • Logging: trace-level log of limb_count in constructors (guarded by feature flag trace-alloc) for allocation profiling in later phases.
  • No migration concerns for this phase — it is the foundation; later phases depend on types being stable.

Phase 2 — addition + subtraction

2.1 Objectives

  • Implement all carry/borrow arithmetic in bn-add: limb-wise addition with carry via u128, in-place add_assign, subtraction with borrow (requires a >= b for BigUint), and the add_mul_1 primitive (a += b * limb).
  • Invariant I3 (carry conservation): no carry or borrow may be dropped; result length grows by at most 1 on addition.
  • Subtraction contract: only valid when self >= rhs; caller must check before calling; returns exact magnitude.
  • add_mul_1 is the inner loop of schoolbook multiplication; it must be allocation-minimal.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

bn-add (src/lib.rs) — signatures (no bodies):

// Add b into a (in-place); returns carry out (0 or 1).
pub fn add_limbs_in_place(a: LimbsMut<'_>, b: Limbs<'_>) -> Limb;

// Extend a by one limb if carry is nonzero.
pub fn add_assign(lhs: &mut BigUint, rhs: &BigUint);

// Requires lhs >= rhs (panic in debug, UB in release if violated).
pub fn sub_assign(lhs: &mut BigUint, rhs: &BigUint);

// a += b * c; returns carry out of the top limb.
pub fn add_mul_1(a: LimbsMut<'_>, b: Limbs<'_>, c: Limb) -> Limb;

// Single-limb addition: a += x; returns carry.
pub fn add_1(a: LimbsMut<'_>, x: Limb) -> Limb;

// Single-limb subtraction: a -= x; requires a >= x.
pub fn sub_1(a: LimbsMut<'_>, x: Limb) -> Limb;
  • add_limbs_in_place: for i in 0..min(len_a,len_b), sum:u128 = a[i]+b[i]+carry; low 64 bits → a[i], high 64 bits → carry; if b is longer, keep propagating carry through its remaining limbs; push final carry as a new limb if nonzero.
  • sub_assign: for i in 0..len_b, diff:i128 = a[i]-b[i]-borrow; low bits → a[i], borrow = 1 if negative; propagate borrow through remaining a limbs; debug-assert final borrow == 0 (else a < b); normalize lhs after (strip trailing zero limbs).
  • add_mul_1: for i in 0..len_b, prod:u128 = a[i]+b[i]*c+carry; low bits → a[i], high bits → carry; a must be pre-sized to ≥ len_b limbs by caller; returns carry for caller to push.
  • BigUint operators (bn-uint, delegate to bn-add): Add allocates max(len_a,len_b)+1 limbs then normalizes; Sub requires self >= rhs (debug panic otherwise); AddAssign/SubAssign reuse existing storage.
  • Anchors: I3 (carry/borrow never lost — captured in u128 per P3); P11 (aliasing safety — add_limbs_in_place/sub_assign read each limb exactly once before writing it, making them alias-safe by construction for a += &a / a -= &a).

2.3 Deliverable

  • bn-add crate compiles and all six functions pass unit tests.
  • BigUint supports +, -, +=, -= operators.
  • add_mul_1 usable by Phase 4 schoolbook multiply.
  • Carry/borrow never lost for inputs up to 10 000-limb operands (stress test passes).

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
a + b == b + a (commutativity) 10 000 proptest cases
(a + b) - b == a where a + b >= b 10 000 proptest cases
Result of add_assign length <= max(len_a,len_b)+1 all proptest cases
Subtraction panics in debug when rhs > lhs explicit test
add_mul_1 result matches a + b * c computed via u128 for 1-limb 10 000 proptest cases
No trailing-zero limbs on any returned BigUint property test (I1)

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected
fixtures/add/add_vectors.json Hex pairs (a, b) + hex sum a + b matches
fixtures/add/sub_vectors.json Triples (a, b, diff) with a >= b a - b matches
fixtures/add/add_mul1_vectors.json (a, b, c, result) one-limb scale add_mul_1 result matches
fixtures/add/carry_propagation.json Cases requiring multi-limb carry chain correct carry propagation
fixtures/add/sub_underflow.json Cases where a < b debug panic / error in test

2.6 Pitfalls

  • Carry dropped at boundary: when b is longer than a, iteration stops before propagating carry from b's high limbs. Root cause: loop terminates at min(len_a, len_b). Fix: continue iterating b's remaining limbs adding carry.
  • add_mul_1 accumulator overflow: a[i] + b[i]*c + carry can reach u64::MAX * u64::MAX + u64::MAX + u64::MAX ≈ 2^128 - 1; a u128 accumulator is tight but sufficient — only correct if no extra addition is added.
  • sub_assign produces non-normalized result: subtraction may leave zero high limbs; normalize must be called after. Symptom: subsequent length-based comparisons are wrong.
  • Borrow sign extension: if diff is computed as u64 with wrapping subtraction, borrow extraction differs from i128 path; mixing the two produces incorrect borrow. Lock on the i128 path throughout.
  • Length not reserved before add_mul_1: the output slice must be pre-sized; if a.len() < b.len(), index-out-of-bounds in release (UB if using get_unchecked). Caller contract must be documented and enforced.

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • Inner loop of add_limbs_in_place is the hot path for all multi-precision arithmetic; keep it branch-minimal; avoid bounds checks with explicit slice lengths.
  • add_mul_1 is called n times in schoolbook multiply (Phase 4); any allocation inside it is catastrophic — it operates on LimbsMut slices only, no Vec access.
  • add_assign reuses the existing allocation of lhs.limbs where lhs has sufficient capacity; use Vec::resize to extend to rhs.len() + 1 before the loop.
  • Compiler should auto-vectorize the add_limbs_in_place loop if written without data-dependent branches; inspect LLVM IR in CI with RUSTFLAGS=--emit=llvm-ir.

2.8 Edge Cases

Input Expected
zero + zero zero (empty limbs)
a + 0 clone of a, normalized
a - a zero (empty limbs)
Maximum u64 limb + 1 carry into new limb
Very long a, very short b (single limb) correct carry propagation
add_mul_1 with c = 0 a unchanged, carry = 0
add_mul_1 with c = u64::MAX maximum accumulator value, no overflow

2.9 Failure Analysis

  • Silent carry loss (I3 violation): addition of equal-length operands that both have high bits set produces a length-n result missing the carry limb. Detection: proptest (a + b).bit_len() >= max(a.bit_len(), b.bit_len()) when both inputs are nonzero.
  • Debug panic on subtraction underflow: blast radius is a panic only in debug; release silently wraps. Ensure subtraction callers always check a >= b first (enforced in BigUint::sub impl).
  • Operator += on aliased data: Rust ownership prevents aliasing; no TOCTOU risk. Document that add_assign may not alias source and destination (enforced by borrow checker).
  • Postmortem: failing (a+b)-b == a proptest is the primary signal for both carry and borrow bugs.

2.10 Operational Notes

  • Add a #[cfg(debug_assertions)] check at the top of sub_assign that panics with "bn-add: subtraction underflow: lhs < rhs" and hex-dumps both operand lengths.
  • Expose add_limbs_in_place and add_mul_1 as pub(crate) only; they are kernel primitives, not public API.
  • Benchmark add_assign on 1k-, 10k-, 100k-limb inputs in bn-add/benches/add.rs using criterion; track regression.
  • No migration concerns: addition and subtraction are foundational; no external consumers until Phase 4.

Phase 3 — shifts + bitwise + bit utilities

3.1 Objectives

  • Implement bit-level operations on BigUint in bn-uint: left/right shift by arbitrary bit count, bit_len, count_ones, trailing_zeros, single-bit set/test/clear.
  • These operations are prerequisites for: binary GCD (Phase 11, uses trailing_zeros), division normalization (Phase 7, uses shift-left to set top bit), and windowed modpow (Phase 11, uses test_bit).
  • No new crates; all in bn-uint. Shifts must not corrupt limb count or violate I1.
  • Constraint: shift-by-0 is a no-op; shift-right on zero returns zero; shift-left on zero returns zero.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

bn-uint — shift operations

Signatures:

pub fn shl_assign(lhs: &mut BigUint, bits: u64);
pub fn shr_assign(lhs: &mut BigUint, bits: u64);
pub fn shl(lhs: &BigUint, bits: u64) -> BigUint;
pub fn shr(lhs: &BigUint, bits: u64) -> BigUint;
  • shl_assign: split limb_shift = bits/64, bit_shift = bits%64; pre-insert limb_shift zero limbs (limbs = [0;limb_shift] ++ limbs); if bit_shift>0, carry bits low-to-high (carry = limb >> (64-bit_shift) from previous, limb = (limb<<bit_shift)|carry_from_prev), pushing a new high limb if carry survives; normalize.
  • shr_assign: same split; drain the bottom limb_shift limbs; if bit_shift>0, carry bits high-to-low (carry = limb << (64-bit_shift) from previous, limb = (limb>>bit_shift)|carry_from_above); normalize.

bn-uint — bit utilities

pub fn bit_len(x: &BigUint) -> u64;          // see Phase 1
pub fn count_ones(x: &BigUint) -> u64;        // sum of popcount over all limbs
pub fn trailing_zeros(x: &BigUint) -> Option<u64>; // None if x==0
pub fn test_bit(x: &BigUint, n: u64) -> bool; // bit n (0 = LSB)
pub fn set_bit(x: &mut BigUint, n: u64);      // set bit n; extend limbs if needed
pub fn clear_bit(x: &mut BigUint, n: u64);    // clear bit n; normalize afterward
  • trailing_zeros: scan limbs low-to-high; first nonzero limb at index i gives i*64 + limb.trailing_zeros(); None if all limbs zero (x == 0).
  • set_bit: limb_idx = n/64, bit_idx = n%64; extend limbs to limb_idx+1 with zeros if necessary; limbs[limb_idx] |= 1u64 << bit_idx; no normalization needed (only adds high bits).
  • clear_bit: no-op if limb_idx >= limbs.len() (bit already 0); else limbs[limb_idx] &= !(1u64 << bit_idx); normalize (high limb may become zero).
  • BigUint operator impls: impl Shl<u64>, impl Shr<u64>, impl ShlAssign<u64>, impl ShrAssign<u64>.
  • Anchors: I1 (canonical magnitude — shr_assign/clear_bit may zero the top limb and must normalize() afterward); P2 (canonical representation).

3.3 Deliverable

  • All shift and bit-utility functions compile and pass unit + property tests.
  • trailing_zeros usable by Phase 11 binary GCD.
  • shl_assign usable by Phase 7 Knuth normalization step.
  • test_bit usable by Phase 11 windowed modpow.
  • Benchmark: shl on 10k-limb input < 50 µs on development hardware.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
x >> k << k == x & ~((1 << k) - 1) (round-trip drops low bits) 10 000 proptest cases
x << k >> k == x when k < x.trailing_zeros() 5 000 proptest cases
(x << k).bit_len() == x.bit_len() + k for nonzero x 5 000 proptest cases
trailing_zeros(x << k) == trailing_zeros(x) + k for nonzero x 5 000 proptest cases
test_bit(x, n) == (x >> n) & 1 == 1 10 000 proptest cases
set_bit then test_bit at same position returns true unit test
clear_bit then test_bit at same position returns false unit test
No trailing zero limbs after any shift property test (I1)

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected
fixtures/bits/shl_vectors.json (hex, shift_amount, hex_result) shl matches
fixtures/bits/shr_vectors.json (hex, shift_amount, hex_result) shr matches
fixtures/bits/trailing_zeros.json (hex, expected_tz or null for zero) matches
fixtures/bits/bit_len.json (hex, expected_bit_len) exact match
fixtures/bits/set_clear_bit.json (hex, bit_pos, set_result, clear_result) matches
fixtures/bits/shl_zero.json Shift of zero by various amounts always zero

3.6 Pitfalls

  • shl by multiple of 64: bit_shift = 0; the bit-shift loop must be skipped entirely; if it runs with 64 - 0 = 64, Rust panics on u64::overflowing_shr(64) (shift amount equals word size). Fix: guard the inner loop with if bit_shift > 0.
  • shr leaving un-normalized high limbs: after bit-shifting right, the top limb may become zero. Normalize must follow. Symptom: bit_len over-reports.
  • set_bit on index beyond current length without extending: panics via index-out-of-bounds in debug; UB with get_unchecked in release. Fix: limbs.resize(limb_idx + 1, 0) before ORing.
  • trailing_zeros returning 0 for zero-valued BigUint: should return None; returning Some(0) causes binary GCD to spin on a zero input. Root cause: loop exits early on limbs.is_empty().
  • Off-by-one in shl carry: carry = limb >> (64 - bit_shift) when bit_shift == 0 must be 0, not garbage. The guard if bit_shift > 0 prevents the shift by 64.
  • High-to-low vs low-to-high iteration order: shl must propagate carry from low to high; iterating high-to-low drops carry. shr must propagate from high to low.

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • shl_assign by large limb_shift: the insert(0, 0) approach is O(n * limb_shift) due to repeated moves; use Vec::extend + rotate, or allocate a new vec of the final size and copy. Preferred: allocate output vec of size old_len + limb_shift + 1, copy shifted, no insert.
  • shr_assign by large limb_shift: draining from the front is O(n) per drain; instead, slice the tail: limbs = limbs[limb_shift..].to_vec().
  • count_ones: trivially parallelizable over limbs; no optimization needed for correctness phase, but avoid unnecessary copies.
  • Large shift benchmarks (100k-limb shl 32768): should complete in < 5 ms; track in bn-uint/benches/bits.rs.

3.8 Edge Cases

Input Expected
shl(zero, k) for any k zero
shr(zero, k) for any k zero
shl(x, 0) clone of x
shr(x, x.bit_len()) zero
shr(x, x.bit_len() + 1000) zero (no panic)
shl(x, u64::MAX) valid (large allocation; test with small x only)
trailing_zeros(zero) None
test_bit(x, n) for n >= x.bit_len() false (no panic)
clear_bit(x, n) for n >= x.bit_len() no-op, no panic

3.9 Failure Analysis

  • Shift-by-word-size panic: u64::wrapping_shr(64) is well-defined in Rust (returns the original value), but 64u64 >> 64 via the >> operator panics in debug with "attempt to shift right with overflow". Manifestation: CI fails on shl by multiples of 64. Detection: run tests with RUSTFLAGS="-C debug-assertions=yes". Recovery: guard all shifts of the form 64 - bit_shift behind if bit_shift > 0.
  • I1 violation after shr: normalization omission causes length-inflated BigUint; comparison and multiplication produce wrong answers silently in release. Detection: is_normalized property test.
  • Allocation failure on extreme shl: shl(x, 2^50) would allocate petabytes; guard with a configurable MAX_BIT_LEN (default 2^31 bits / ~256 MB) and return Err or panic with informative message.
  • Postmortem signal: failing round-trip property test x >> k << k == x & mask in CI.

3.10 Operational Notes

  • bit_len is re-used from Phase 1 without change; no duplication.
  • Add trace-level log of (limb_shift, bit_shift) in debug builds of shl_assign; disable in release.
  • Expose trailing_zeros and test_bit as pub in bn-uint; they are used across multiple crates.
  • No deployment or migration concern: this is a library phase; no persistent state or serialization formats.
  • Benchmark target files: bn-uint/benches/bits.rs; criterion groups: shl_1k_limbs, shr_1k_limbs, trailing_zeros_1k_limbs.

Phase 4 — Schoolbook Multiplication + Squaring

4.1 Objectives

  • Implement O(n*m) schoolbook multiply in bn-mul as the correctness oracle for all faster algorithms.
  • Implement dedicated squaring (~2x cheaper than general multiply) exploiting diagonal + doubled off-diagonal structure.
  • Result length invariant: |a| + |b| limbs exactly before normalization.
  • Expose mul_schoolbook(a: Limbs, b: Limbs, out: LimbsMut) and sqr_schoolbook(a: Limbs, out: LimbsMut) as canonical reference kernels.
  • No unsafe: all overflow captured in DoubleLimb (u128); no wrapping_* shortcuts that could silently lose a carry.
  • These kernels are the permanent oracle; they must remain callable even when Karatsuba/Toom3/NTT are active (P1, I4).
  • Establish MulAlgo enum in bn-mul (extended in Phase 5 and Phase 6) and the mul_dispatch entry point.
  • Constraint: do not import bn-div, bn-mod, or bn-gcd from this phase — multiplication must be leaf-level.
  • Satisfy the add_mul_1 contract used by Phase 7 (Knuth Division): a += b * single_limb via schoolbook scalar path.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files: bn-mul/src/schoolbook.rs, bn-mul/src/sqr.rs, bn-mul/src/lib.rs, bn-mul/tests/schoolbook_tests.rs

Algorithm — general multiply:

  • Allocate zero-initialized out: Vec<Limb> of length n+m; nested loop i in 0..n, j in 0..m accumulating acc: DoubleLimb = out[i+j] as u128 + a[i] as u128 * b[j] as u128 + carry, storing (acc & 0xFFFF_FFFF_FFFF_FFFF) as Limb into out[i+j] and carrying acc >> 64.
  • After each inner loop, propagate the residual carry (at most 1: max per-step value (2^64-1)^2 + (2^64-1) + 1 = 2^128 - 2^64 + 1 < 2^128) into out[i+m]; normalize the result (strip trailing zero limbs) via bn-uint::normalize.

Algorithm — squaring:

  • Zero-initialize out: Vec<Limb> of length 2*n; Phase A (diagonal) accumulates d = a[i]*a[i] into out[2i]/out[2i+1] with carry for each i; Phase B (off-diagonal) computes cross = a[i]*a[j] for each i<j, doubles it via (cross, overflow) = cross.overflowing_shl(1) (doubling can overflow u128 at max limb values, so the overflow bit propagates as an extra carry), and accumulates into out[i+j]/out[i+j+1].
  • Invariant: sqr_schoolbook(a) == mul_schoolbook(a, a) for all inputs (enforced by property test, I4 analog for squaring).

Public interface (signatures only):

// bn-mul/src/schoolbook.rs
pub fn mul_schoolbook(a: &[Limb], b: &[Limb]) -> Vec<Limb>;
pub fn sqr_schoolbook(a: &[Limb]) -> Vec<Limb>;

// bn-mul/src/lib.rs
pub fn mul_bigint(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> BigUint;  // dispatches; Phase 4 routes to schoolbook
pub fn sqr_bigint(a: &BigUint) -> BigUint;

Integration with bn-uint:

  • BigUint implements Mul<&BigUint> (calls bn-mul::mul_bigint), MulAssign<&BigUint> (*self = self.clone() * rhs), and square() (calls bn-mul::sqr_bigint); zero operand short-circuits before calling the kernel and returns BigUint::zero().
  • BigUint::from(u64) / from(u128) provide single/double-limb constants used in tests. The add_mul_1(a: &mut BigUint, b: &BigUint, c: Limb) helper in bn-add relies on mul_schoolbook semantics (a += b * c); schoolbook is the basis for this scalar-multiply path.

Test harness bn-mul/tests/schoolbook_tests.rs:

  • Unit: 0*0=0, 0*x=0, 1*x=x, x*1=x, single-limb products; known-value fixture replay from fixtures/mul/schoolbook_basic.json at test startup.
  • Property: a*b == b*a (commutative), a*(b+c) == a*b + a*c (distributive, uses Phase 2 add); squaring agreement sqr(a) == mul(a,a) for sizes 1–64 limbs, 1 000 random inputs; size sweep at every size 1–64 limbs confirms no size-dependent off-by-one.
  • Carry stress: all-0xFFFF_FFFF_FFFF_FFFF inputs, 1–32 limbs; add_mul_1 integration checks bn-add::add_mul_1(a, b, c) result matches a + mul(b, BigUint::from(c)).

4.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p bn-mul green: unit, property, carry-stress, squaring-agreement tests all pass.
  • BigUint::mul and BigUint::square callable end-to-end from bn-uint; Mul and MulAssign traits implemented.
  • bn-cli bench schoolbook --size 32 reports throughput in ns/op.
  • Schoolbook reference binary permanently available as oracle for differential testing by Phases 5, 6, 14; exported as pub fn mul_schoolbook.
  • Result length == |a|+|b| before normalization asserted in debug builds.
  • cargo doc -p bn-mul produces documentation for all public symbols.
  • No unsafe blocks anywhere in bn-mul/src/schoolbook.rs or bn-mul/src/sqr.rs.

4.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Detection
Carry dropped in inner loop Product off by 2^64 per carry event carry accumulated as Limb not DoubleLimb Diff-test with Python; carry stress fixture
Off-by-one in out length Buffer overrun panic or garbage limb out.len() set to n+m-1 Assert out.len()==n+m pre-normalize
Squaring doubles diagonal Result ~2x too large for large inputs a[i]*a[i] included in doubled off-diagonal Squaring vs. multiply agreement test
Normalize skipped I1 violated; downstream compare and hash break Forgot normalize() call after multiply Canonical-form property test on multiply output
u128 cast to u64 truncates Silent data corruption; off by one or more limbs (acc as u64) instead of (acc & MASK) as u64 Overflow-checked build; diff-test any pair
Doubling overflow in squaring Off by 2^128 when cross = 2^127 cross << 1 overflows u128 overflowing_shl path; max-limb squaring test
Zero result not normalized Sign invariant I2 broken in callers mul(0, x) returns [0] instead of [] mul_bigint(BigUint::zero(), x).is_zero() test

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Inner loop is the only hot path: hoist a[i] load outside the inner loop; keep out[i+j] load-modify-store in registers; Vec pre-allocated at exact size n+m (avoids realloc; zero-init required for correct initial carry).
  • For n, m <= 32 limbs both operands fit in L1 cache (256 bytes each) — no cache-miss concern at schoolbook size; debug-mode overflow checks don't interfere since stores are to out[i+j]: Limb while the DoubleLimb path never overflows.
  • Squaring: off-diagonal halving of operations is meaningful (~1.8x empirically), never skip it even for the oracle; outer i 0..n, inner j i+1..n (strict) visits each off-diagonal pair once then doubles, avoiding the n²/2 redundancy of general multiply used as squaring.
  • For very unequal operands (|a|=1, |b|=n): outer loop over the length-1 operand makes this effectively O(n) — critical for bn-add::add_mul_1's a * small_constant pattern; prefer no SIMD in this oracle kernel (clarity over micro-optimization).
  • Allocation dominates for small operands (< 4 limbs); consider a stack-allocated fixed-size array for sizes <= 4 limbs to eliminate heap cost in tight loops.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • a = 0 or b = 0: short-circuit before allocation, return canonical zero; a = 1: result equals b exactly after normalize; both single-limb: result fits in at most two limbs ([low, high] layout verified in unit test).
  • Maximum limb value 0xFFFF_FFFF_FFFF_FFFF in every position exercises full carry chain; sqr(0) == 0, sqr(1) == 1.
  • Very unequal sizes (|a|=1, |b|=1000): iterate outer loop over the shorter operand to minimize stack frame reuse; operand aliasing forbidden — mul_schoolbook must not alias a or b with out (kernel takes slices, caller holds a separate Vec).

4.9 Failure Analysis

  • Wrong product (carry loss): off by a power of 2^64. Detected immediately by diff-test against Python int or Rust num-bigint. Blast radius: all downstream consumers (bn-div, bn-mod, bn-gcd, bn-int, bn-rational). Recovery: fix carry path; re-run full cross-check suite; add the failing pair as a regression fixture.
  • Panic on buffer overrun (out.len() wrong): contained to test/run scope, no silent corruption; recovery: audit all out.len() expressions in schoolbook.rs, add static assertion out.len() == n + m before the loop. I1 violation propagated (normalize skipped): BigUint::cmp/hash produce wrong results for equal magnitudes with different trailing-zero counts, detected by canonical-form property test; recovery: add normalize() call at the end of the kernel wrapper.
  • Squaring off-by-factor-of-2 (doubling applied to the diagonal): detected by squaring-vs-multiply agreement test; recovery: audit Phase A vs Phase B loop boundaries so diagonal terms are never included in the doubled path. Incorrect out zeroing skipped (stale data from unsafely reused buffer): always zero-initialize out at kernel start; document the zeroing requirement in a # Safety comment if unsafe reuse is introduced later.

4.10 Operational Notes

  • MulAlgo::Schoolbook enum variant signals this path, logged at TRACE (tracing::trace!(algo="schoolbook", a_limbs=%a.limbs.len(), b_limbs=%b.limbs.len())); schoolbook must remain callable as oracle for all time — do NOT gate-remove behind a Cargo feature flag.
  • bn-cli bench schoolbook --size N measures in isolation, outputs ns/op and MB/s; --size 8,16,32,64,128 accepts comma-separated sizes for a size sweep and prints a table.
  • No deployment, migration, or I/O implications; pure in-memory computation. Phase 14's differential harness calls mul_schoolbook directly, bypassing dispatch — keep the function pub.
  • cargo bench -p bn-mul --bench schoolbook is the canonical criterion benchmark, added to the CI nightly job; module-level doc comment in bn-mul/src/schoolbook.rs must state: "Reference implementation. Do not optimize at the cost of clarity. This is the oracle."

Phase 5 — Karatsuba + Toom-Cook Multiplication

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement Karatsuba (O(n^1.585)): split each operand at half-length, reduce to 3 recursive half-size multiplies plus additions/subtractions; implement Toom-Cook-3 (O(n^1.465)): 5-point evaluation + 5 pointwise multiplies + Bodrato interpolation with exact integer intermediate arithmetic.
  • Implement threshold dispatch in bn-mul::dispatch: Schoolbook below KARATSUBA_THRESHOLD, Karatsuba below TOOM3_THRESHOLD, Toom3 above (Phase 6 adds Ntt above NTT_THRESHOLD); all three must satisfy I4 (agree with schoolbook on every input); no floating-point in either algorithm — all intermediates are BigUint/BigInt with exact carries. Squaring fast paths for both algorithms (3 sub-multiplications become 3 squarings; 5 multiplies become 5 squarings); thresholds configurable at runtime via env vars for benchmark-driven calibration.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files: bn-mul/src/karatsuba.rs, bn-mul/src/toom3.rs, bn-mul/src/dispatch.rs, bn-mul/src/thresholds.rs Karatsuba (karatsuba.rs): split x,y at half=max(|x|,|y|)/2 limbs (zero-copy slices) into x1*B+x0, y1*B+y0 (B=2^(64*half)); recursively compute z0=x0*y0, z2=x1*y1, and z1_full=(x0+x1)*(y0+y1) (sums may need half+1 limbs), then z1=z1_full-z0-z2 via BigInt subtraction (non-negative for valid inputs, but intermediates can go signed); assemble result=z0+(z1<<half)+(z2<<2*half) via bn-add; recursion bottoms out to Schoolbook at KARATSUBA_THRESHOLD; bound |z0|<=2*half, |z2|<=2*(|x|-half), |z1|<=2*(half+1), assembled <=|x|+|y|+2; normalize at end.

pub fn mul_karatsuba(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> BigUint;
fn karatsuba_inner(a: &[Limb], b: &[Limb]) -> Vec<Limb>;

Toom-Cook-3 (toom3.rs): split at third=ceil(max(|x|,|y|)/3) into x2*B^2+x1*B+x0, y2*B^2+y1*B+y0 (B=2^(64*third)); evaluate both operands at 5 points and take the 5 pointwise products w0,w1,w_1,w_2,w_inf via recursive dispatch:

Point x value (BigInt) y value (BigInt)
0 x0 y0
1 x0+x1+x2 y0+y1+y2
-1 x0-x1+x2 y0-y1+y2
-2 x0-2x1+4x2 y0-2y1+4y2
inf x2 y2
  • Bodrato interpolation recovers r0..r4: r0=w0; r4=w_inf; t1=(w1-w_1)/2; t2=(w1+w_1)/2-r0-r4; t3=(w_2-w1)/3; r3=(t3-t1)/2; r1=t1-r3; r2=t2-r3-r4 (sign-aware); every division (/2,/3) is provably exact — nonzero remainder is an invariant violation, panic with operand dump.
  • Assemble result=r4*B^4+r3*B^3+r2*B^2+r1*B+r0; all evaluation/interpolation values are BigInt (bn-int); squaring (a==b) turns all 5 pointwise products into squarings via a squaring flag passed to recursive dispatch.
pub fn mul_toom3(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> BigUint;
fn toom3_inner(a: &[Limb], b: &[Limb]) -> Vec<Limb>;
fn exact_div2(x: BigInt) -> BigInt;     // panic if remainder != 0
fn exact_div3(x: BigInt) -> BigInt;     // panic if remainder != 0
fn exact_div6(x: BigInt) -> BigInt;     // panic if remainder != 0

Dispatch (dispatch.rs, thresholds.rs):

Constant Default (limbs) Override env
KARATSUBA_THRESHOLD 32 BN_KARATSUBA_THRESHOLD
TOOM3_THRESHOLD 96 BN_TOOM3_THRESHOLD
  • mul_dispatch/sqr_dispatch compare max(a.limbs.len(), b.limbs.len()) to the thresholds, record the selected MulAlgo variant, and delegate (sqr_dispatch reuses the squaring identities above for both algorithms).
  • Test harness: karatsuba_tests.rs/toom3_tests.rs proptest cross-check vs. schoolbook at 32–200/96–600 limbs (middle-term overflow, unequal-length, eval-at-(-2), squaring fast path); dispatch_tests.rs confirms the MulAlgo variant at, below, and above each threshold (±1 boundary cases); all tests re-run in release via cargo test -p bn-mul --release in CI. Anchors: I4 (Karatsuba/Toom3 must agree with schoolbook, verified by the cross-checks above); P4 (dispatch by operand size); P12 (threshold monotonicity — KARATSUBA_THRESHOLD < TOOM3_THRESHOLD enforced in dispatch.rs/thresholds.rs).

5.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p bn-mul passes all cross-check tests for all three algorithms at all size classes; MulAlgo dispatch observable via TRACE logs with each test confirming the expected variant.
  • Thresholds readable/overridable at runtime via env vars without recompile; calibration instructions added to CONTRIBUTING.md.
  • Toom3 interpolation exact-division panics on non-zero remainder with operand dump to WARN log.
  • bn-cli bench mul --algo karatsuba --size 64 / --algo toom3 --size 192 report ns/op; Criterion benchmarks added at bn-mul/benches/karatsuba.rs and toom3.rs.
  • cargo doc -p bn-mul covers all public exports (mul_karatsuba, mul_toom3, mul_dispatch).
