5.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Detection
Middle-term carry limb ignored Karatsuba off by 2^(64*half) x0+x1 allocated without carry limb Cross-check proptest
Toom3 inexact division Panic or silent wrong result Interpolation formula error or incorrect shift Assert remainder == 0 in each exact-div call
Threshold off-by-one Wrong algo at boundary < vs <= in dispatch Boundary fixture tests
Unequal-length split miscalculation Wrong sub-problem sizes Half computed from ` a
Signed intermediate using BigUint Panic or wrong sign in assembly z1 = z1_full - z0 - z2 can go negative Type-level: force BigInt; test with unequal operands
Recursion stack overflow Crash on adversarial deep input Threshold too small Set minimum recursion guard: if len <= 4 { schoolbook }
Memory spike per recursion OOM on large inputs Allocating new bufs at each level Pre-allocate working buffers; reuse across recursive calls
Squaring path bypassed Slower than necessary Squaring dispatch missing in Karatsuba/Toom3 Benchmark sqr vs mul(a,a)

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • Cost: Karatsuba = 3 recursive multiplies + 4 add/sub + 2 shifts + 2 adds (add/sub overhead dominates at small sizes, tune threshold per hardware); Toom3 = 5 multiplies + ~30 cheap add/sub/shift/exact-div per level (constants 2,3,6 divide in one pass), worthwhile only above ~96 limbs — the bottleneck in both is the sub-multiplications, not the evaluation arithmetic.
  • Buffer reuse: preallocate z0,z1,z2 (and the half+1-limb x0+x1 sum) at max size before recursion; never allocate inside inner loops; pass mutable buffer references down the recursion stack.
  • Squaring: z0=sqr(x0), z2=sqr(x1), z1_full=sqr(x0+x1) — 3 squarings replace 2 squarings + 1 multiply.
  • Stack depth: Karatsuba log2(n/KARATSUBA_THRESHOLD) levels (~8 at 10 000 limbs), Toom3 log3(n/TOOM3_THRESHOLD) (~5) — both safe at ~100 bytes/frame.
  • Measure with criterion at sizes [32,64,96,128,192,256,512] limbs via BenchmarkGroup; cache fit: Karatsuba @64 limbs = 512 B (L1), Toom3 @192 limbs ≈ 4.6 KB (L1); NTT crossover sits well above L2 saturation.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • Unequal lengths: split at max(|a|,|b|) / 2; shorter operand zero-pads conceptually (its high slice is empty/zero; BigUint::zero() returned for that slice).
  • Single-limb inputs: must fall through to Schoolbook; do not recurse into Karatsuba or Toom3 — degenerate split leads to infinite recursion or empty slices.
  • Power-of-2 limb counts: Karatsuba splits evenly — no special case.
  • Odd limb counts: unequal split (half = n/2, n - half); lower half may be one limb shorter — handle in slice operations; test with odd-sized inputs explicitly.
  • Zero in one operand: short-circuit to zero before splitting; avoid degenerate recursion; result must be canonical zero.
  • a == b pointer equality or value equality: both squaring dispatch and general multiply produce the same result; squaring dispatch is preferred for performance; implement pointer-equality fast path in sqr_dispatch.
  • Toom3 evaluation at -2: x0 - 2*x1 + 4*x2 can have large magnitude; allocate BigInt with sufficient limbs; 4*x2 can be up to 4 * (2^64)^third which still fits in BigInt with third+1 limbs.
  • Exact div by 3 in Toom3 interpolation: intermediate that is not divisible by 3 signals a bug in evaluation; assert remainder == 0 in both debug and release builds.
  • Toom3 x2 slice shorter than third: when |x| is not exactly 3*third, the top slice is shorter; treat missing limbs as zero; do not panic on short-slice access.
  • Karatsuba with |a| = 1, |b| = KARATSUBA_THRESHOLD + 1: technically above threshold but only one limb on one side — dispatch to schoolbook for single-limb operands regardless of threshold.

5.9 Failure Analysis

  • I4 violation (wrong product): Detected by cross-check proptest. Blast radius: all code using BigUint::mul at sizes above threshold: bn-div (Newton-reciprocal, Knuth for large), bn-mod (Barrett/Montgomery), bn-rational (cross-multiply). Recovery: bisect threshold to isolate the failing size class; add the failing pair as a regression fixture; revert threshold as stop-gap.
  • Panic in Toom3 interpolation (non-exact div): Blast radius: only the large-multiply path. Operator visibility: panic message includes operand sizes and the non-zero remainder value. Recovery: log raw operands in debug build; reproduce with bn-cli calc --algo toom3 <a> <b>; file bug with the fixture; temporarily reduce TOOM3_THRESHOLD via env to bypass.
  • Karatsuba middle-term sign error: z1 = z1_full - z0 - z2 goes negative due to bug; result is massively wrong (wraps in BigUint). Detected by cross-check proptest within the first few cases. Recovery: switch to BigInt for z1; ensure sign is handled before shifting into result.
  • Threshold regression (perf degrades): Detected by criterion CI run flagging > 5% throughput drop. Recovery: revert thresholds.rs constant; re-calibrate on target hardware.
  • Recursion bottoms out at wrong threshold: sub-problem dispatched back through Toom3 instead of schoolbook. Detected via infinite recursion / stack overflow on specific sizes. Recovery: add explicit guard if max_limbs < KARATSUBA_THRESHOLD { return mul_schoolbook(...) } at entry of each recursive function.

5.10 Operational Notes

  • MulAlgo variant logged at TRACE on every dispatch call (tracing::trace!(algo=?selected, a_limbs, b_limbs)); collect via RUST_LOG=bn_mul=trace to confirm dispatch on real workloads; tracing::span! wraps each Karatsuba/Toom3 call at DEBUG for flamegraph profiling (cargo flamegraph).
  • bn-cli bench mul --algo karatsuba --size 64, --algo toom3 --size 192, or --algo all --size 8,16,32,64,96,128,192,256,512 (full comparison table) for targeted and calibration measurement.
  • Threshold constants live in bn-mul/src/thresholds.rs; calibrate per deployment hardware and document calibrated values in CONTRIBUTING.md.
  • Criterion benchmarks in bn-mul/benches/ run nightly in CI; regressions flagged when throughput drops > 5% vs. baseline.
  • Toom3 exact-division failure produces a WARN log with hex-encoded operands for offline debugging; never silently returns a wrong result.
  • No I/O, persistence, or migration concerns (pure computation); module docs in karatsuba.rs/toom3.rs must cite Brent & Zimmermann (Modern Computer Arithmetic) and Bodrato's Toom-Cook paper.

Phase 6 — NTT Multiplication for Huge Operands

6.1 Objectives

  • Implement number-theoretic transform (NTT) multiplication in bn-fftmul for operands above NTT_THRESHOLD limbs, using integer-only arithmetic over NTT-friendly prime moduli — no f32/f64 at any step (P5, I8).
  • CRT recombination across multiple primes recovers the exact integer product with zero rounding; forward NTT, inverse NTT, pointwise multiply, and CRT combine are implemented as separate testable units.
  • Integrate with bn-mul dispatch: MulAlgo::Ntt selected above NTT_THRESHOLD; result must satisfy I4 (agree with schoolbook on all inputs) and I8 (zero rounding error by construction).
  • Squaring fast path sqr_ntt(a) avoids computing two identical forward transforms; mul_ntt returns Result<BigUint, BigNumError> to allow graceful fallback on oversized inputs.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Files: bn-fftmul/src/ntt.rs, bn-fftmul/src/primes.rs, bn-fftmul/src/crt.rs, bn-fftmul/src/plan.rs, bn-fftmul/src/lib.rs, bn-fftmul/tests/ntt_tests.rs NTT primes (primes.rs): 3 primes of the form p=c*2^k+1 (each 62-63 bits, subgroup of order 2^k for fast butterflies) with P0*P1*P2 > max_product_coefficient ((2^32-1)^2 * n_limbs_after_split, since limbs split to 32-bit halves before the transform); stored as pub const NTT_PRIMES: [(u64, u64, usize); 3] = (p, primitive_root, max_transform_length); primality/root order verified at test time, not runtime; module doc records the selection rationale and how to add a 4th prime later without breaking callers. NTT plan (plan.rs):

pub struct NttPlan {
    pub primes: [u64; 3],
    pub roots:  [u64; 3],   // primitive 2^k-th roots of unity mod each prime
    pub len: usize,          // power-of-2 transform length
}
pub fn plan_ntt(a_limbs: usize, b_limbs: usize) -> NttPlan;

len = next power of 2 >= a_limbs+b_limbs+1 (limb counts doubled after 32-bit split); asserts len <= max_transform_length per prime, else typed Err(NttTooLarge); plan logged at DEBUG (primes, len, input sizes). Forward/inverse NTT (ntt.rs): Cooley-Tukey iterative NTT, bit-reversed input → natural-order output; modular butterfly (a+b*w) mod p / (a-b*w) mod p in u64 with u128 intermediates; twiddle factors precomputed once per plan_ntt call into a flat Vec<u64> and cached in the plan; bit-reversal permutation applied before log2(len) butterfly passes (stride halves each pass); modular add/sub avoids division via a+b-p*((a+b>=p) as u64); inverse NTT = forward NTT with conjugate (inverse-power) roots, then scale by len.modinv(p) (via Fermat: len.pow(p-2, p), cached in the plan).

pub fn ntt_forward(buf: &mut [u64], p: u64, twiddles: &[u64]);
pub fn ntt_inverse(buf: &mut [u64], p: u64, inv_twiddles: &[u64], len_inv: u64);
pub fn bit_reverse_permute(buf: &mut [u64]);

Limb decomposition: each Limb (u64) splits into two 32-bit halves before the transform (low at 2i, high at 2i+1), bounding each coefficient to (2^32-1)^2*len; after inverse NTT + CRT, halves are re-accumulated with carry back into Vec<Limb>. CRT recombination (crt.rs): Garner's algorithm over the 3 residues recovers each true coefficient as a u128 (fits by construction of the prime product); debug builds assert r[i] < primes[i] and recovered coefficient <= max_coefficient.

pub fn crt_combine(r: [u64; 3], primes: [u64; 3], precomp: &CrtPrecomp) -> u128;
pub struct CrtPrecomp { /* modular inverses: P0^-1 mod P1, (P0*P1)^-1 mod P2, P0*P1, P0*P1*P2 */ }
pub fn precompute_crt(primes: [u64; 3]) -> CrtPrecomp;

End-to-end multiply (lib.rs):

pub fn mul_ntt(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> Result<BigUint, BigNumError>;

Call sequence: plan via plan_ntt (Err(NttTooLarge) if oversized) → decompose a,b into zero-padded 32-bit element arrays of length plan.len → for each of the 3 primes, reduce both arrays mod the prime, forward-NTT each, pointwise-multiply (u128 product mod p), inverse-NTT the product → CRT-combine the 3 per-position residues into a u128 coefficient → re-accumulate 32-bit halves with carry into Vec<Limb> → BigUint::from_limbs(...).normalize(). Dispatch integration (bn-mul/src/dispatch.rs):

Constant Default (limbs) Override env
NTT_THRESHOLD 1024 BN_NTT_THRESHOLD
  • mul_dispatch/sqr_dispatch route to bn_fftmul::mul_ntt/sqr_ntt when max_limbs >= NTT_THRESHOLD (logged at TRACE as MulAlgo::Ntt); NttTooLarge falls back to Toom3 with a WARN log — multiplication is always available, never surfaces an error to the caller. All four dispatch paths (Schoolbook, Karatsuba, Toom3, Ntt) are covered by dispatch integration tests. Test harness (ntt_tests.rs): forward/inverse roundtrip (1 000 random vectors per prime); twiddle correctness vs. fixture/ntt/twiddle_correctness.json for len=8,16,32,64; cross-check mul_ntt == mul_schoolbook on 5 000 pairs at 512–4 096 limbs; squaring cross-check sqr_ntt(a) == mul_schoolbook(a,a) on 2 000 large inputs; CRT vectors from a Python reference (1 000 cases); NttTooLarge path asserted for oversized plan_ntt inputs; CI grep f32|f64 over bn-fftmul/src/ must be empty; full suite re-run via cargo test -p bn-fftmul --release. Anchors: I8 (NTT exactness — CRT recombination in crt.rs must reproduce the exact integer coefficient, zero rounding); P5 (integer-exact transforms — no f32/f64 anywhere in ntt.rs/crt.rs, enforced by the CI grep above); P12 (NTT_THRESHOLD is the top rung of the monotonic multiply threshold ladder).

6.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p bn-fftmul green: forward/inverse roundtrip, cross-check vs. schoolbook, CRT vector tests; mul_ntt callable from bn-mul dispatch for operands >= NTT_THRESHOLD with MulAlgo::Ntt and NttPlan logged at DEBUG on every large multiply.
  • bn-cli bench mul --algo ntt --size 2048 reports ns/op and MB/s; Criterion benchmark added at bn-fftmul/benches/ntt_mul.rs covering sizes 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192 limbs.
  • CI step grep -rE 'f32|f64|f32::|f64::' bn-fftmul/src/ enforced and must return empty; NttTooLarge error variant added to BigNumError with mul_dispatch falling back to Toom3 on this error via a WARN log.
  • cargo doc -p bn-fftmul covers NttPlan, mul_ntt, CrtPrecomp, ntt_forward, ntt_inverse.

6.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Detection
CRT modulus product too small Coefficients wrap; wrong product Primes chosen without bounding max coefficient Debug assert in crt_combine; max-coeff-stress fixture
Twiddle root incorrect order NTT produces garbage; cross-check fails Wrong primitive root or wrong power exponent Forward/inverse roundtrip test per prime
Bit-reversal permutation omitted NTT output scrambled for all inputs Cooley-Tukey requires bit-reversed permutation Roundtrip test catches any order defect
Inverse NTT missing len^{-1} scaling Result off by factor of len Modular inverse of len not applied in inverse Forward/inverse roundtrip: inv(fwd(x)) == x
Carry re-accumulation overflow Limb reconstruction incorrect Coefficient sum during re-accumulation exceeds u128 Diff-test; all-max-limb fixture
32-bit half packing off-by-one Corrupted elements at every other position Index arithmetic error in decompose/reassemble Single-limb cross-check; a=[1]*512 type inputs
NTT_THRESHOLD too low Correct but slower than Toom3 Performance only (not correctness) Benchmark at threshold boundary
Squaring missed (two identical forwards) 2x wasted computation Dispatch does not detect a == b (pointer) for NTT Squaring benchmark vs. mul(a,a)

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • NTT is O(n log n); crossover with Toom3 occurs near 1 024 limbs — calibrate NTT_THRESHOLD empirically per target architecture.
  • Memory: 3 primes × 2 input buffers + 3 result buffers = 9*len u64/call (576 KB at len=8192); allocate all buffers once before the prime loop, never inside it. Twiddle tables are computed once per plan and cached in NttPlan — recomputing per call would add unnecessary O(n log n) overhead.
  • Squaring: sqr_ntt(a) computes one forward NTT of a, pointwise-squares each element (buf[j]=buf[j]^2 mod p), then inverse-NTTs — eliminating one of the two forward transforms.
  • Cache: transform buffers of 8 192 u64 (64 KB) exceed L1; structure butterfly inner loops for sequential (stride-1) access to minimize misses. u128 modular multiply ((a*b) % p, no hardware mulmod, ~3 instructions on x86-64) is the dominant inner-loop op and drives most NTT runtime.
  • CRT is embarrassingly parallelizable per coefficient (noted for Phase 14, not required here); limb decomposition/reassembly is a linear O(n) pre/post pass — fold the mod-p reduction into the decompose step rather than a separate pass.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Input length not a power of 2: zero-pad to next power of 2; padding elements are zero and must not contribute to the product.
  • One operand much smaller than the other: pad shorter operand to len; NTT length set by the longer operand; result is still correct.
  • a == b (squaring via NTT): detect by pointer equality or flag; forward-transform once, pointwise square each element (v[j] = v[j]^2 mod p), inverse NTT; eliminates one forward NTT.
  • Product limb count may exceed both operands' counts by one: carry chain after CRT can extend output by 1 limb beyond a_limbs + b_limbs; len must be at least a_elems + b_elems + 1 after decomposition; verify in plan_ntt.
  • NTT_THRESHOLD overridden to 0 via env: guard with max(NTT_THRESHOLD, TOOM3_THRESHOLD + 1) in dispatch to prevent degenerate routing.
  • Transform length len overflow: assert len < 2^30 in plan_ntt (a safety ceiling); return NttTooLarge for anything larger.
  • Very long operands (1 000 000+ limbs): may exceed max_transform_length; mul_dispatch falls back to Toom3 on NttTooLarge with a WARN log; document the upper-bound limitation.
  • Single-limb inputs routed to NTT (if threshold set to 0 by mistake): result must still be correct; cross-check test covers this for any threshold setting.
  • Twiddle table length mismatch: if len changes between twiddle precomputation and NTT call, butterfly passes access out-of-bounds; assert twiddles.len() == len at entry to ntt_forward.

6.9 Failure Analysis

  • Wrong product (I4/I8 violated): Incorrect CRT, wrong twiddle factor, or missing bit-reversal permutation. Detected by cross-check proptest. Blast radius: all large-multiply consumers: bn-div (Newton division for large operands), bn-mod (Barrett reduction of huge values), bn-rational (large numerator products), bn-mod windowed modpow. Recovery steps: (1) reduce NTT_THRESHOLD via env to bypass NTT and unblock callers; (2) enable RUST_LOG=bn_fftmul=debug and inspect NttPlan for correct primes and roots; (3) compare per-prime inverse NTT output against a reference (Python's ntt or known-good test vector); (4) add failing pair as a regression fixture.
  • CRT overflow silent in release: Debug assert not triggered in --release. Wrong product propagates silently through all downstream users. Mitigation: run cross-check tests in release CI, not only debug; add a 1-in-1000 sampling check in crt_combine release builds that verifies the reconstructed coefficient via a secondary slower path.
  • OOM on huge operands: 9*len u64 (3 primes × 3 buffers) for len=2^21 is ~144 MB — borderline on constrained systems. Detect with allocator instrumentation (cargo bench --features track-alloc); document upper operand size limit in crate-level doc; return NttTooLarge error early before allocating.
  • NttTooLarge unhandled by caller: Panic at call site if error is not propagated. Mitigation: mul_ntt returns Result<BigUint, BigNumError>; mul_dispatch catches NttTooLarge and falls back to Toom3 with a WARN log, never panics.
  • Primitive root wrong order: Forward NTT produces incorrect output; cross-check fails for all inputs above threshold. Detection: root^len != 1 mod p test at startup in debug build; roundtrip test in CI. Recovery: correct the NTT_PRIMES constant.

6.10 Operational Notes

  • NttPlan logged at DEBUG (tracing::debug!(primes=?plan.primes, len=plan.len, a_limbs, b_limbs)); enable via RUST_LOG=bn_fftmul=debug in bn-cli or tests.
  • bn-cli bench mul --algo ntt --size <N> (comma-separated sizes for threshold sweep) or --algo all --size 512,1024,2048,4096,8192 for a cross-algorithm comparison table used in threshold calibration.
  • NTT_THRESHOLD tunable via BN_NTT_THRESHOLD without recompile; persist the calibrated value in deployment notes alongside Karatsuba/Toom3 thresholds.
  • Cross-check tests must run in release CI, not only debug (cargo test -p bn-fftmul --release -- --nocapture after every merge), to catch silent CRT overflow.
  • No I/O, persistence, or migration concerns; pure computation crate with no external dependencies beyond bn-uint, bn-core, bn-mul. Phase 14's differential harness calls mul_ntt directly to verify I8 — keep it pub and never gate-remove it.
  • tracing::span! at INFO for calls with a_limbs > 4096 enables latency tracking on production workloads (e.g., RSA key generation from portfolio projects #89/90); NttTooLarge fallback logged at WARN with operand sizes so operators can detect when the prime set needs extension.

Phase 7 — Division: Knuth Algorithm D

7.1 Objectives

  • Implement multi-precision division in bn-div producing DivRem { quotient: BigUint, remainder: BigUint } satisfying I5: a == q*b + r, 0 <= r < b, b != 0 (typed error on zero); primary algorithm is Knuth Vol 2 Algorithm D — normalize divisor/dividend by left-shifting until the divisor's MSB is set, then estimate one quotient digit per step from the top two limbs of the working dividend.
  • Single-limb fast path: O(n) pass with u128 carry, no normalization overhead, used whenever b.limbs.len() == 1; two-limb divisor shortcut routes the common 128-bit-modulus crypto case to a specialized path.
  • Public surface: div_rem, div_rem_1, divides, and derived div / rem / mod_floor / div_floor.
  • Constraint: no dependency on bn-mul in this phase; Phase 8 adds the Newton path that uses multiplication.

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Module layout:

bn-div/
  src/
    lib.rs          — public API re-exports
    knuth.rs        — Algorithm D: normalization, per-digit estimation, multiply-subtract, add-back
    single.rs       — single-limb and two-limb fast paths
    thresholds.rs   — NEWTON_THRESHOLD const (used by Phase 8)
    error.rs        — DivByZero error type (re-exported from bn-core or defined here)
  benches/
    knuth.rs        — size-sweep benchmarks
  tests/
    conformance.rs  — fixture-driven tests

Normalization (knuth.rs): normalization_shift(d) = d.limbs.last().unwrap().leading_zeros(); normalize(src, shift) returns a shifted copy (cheap clone if shift==0), asserting result.last() >= (1u64<<63) in debug; dividend normalization produces u_norm with one extra high limb to capture bits shifted out of a. Quotient-digit estimation (knuth.rs): for j from q_len-1 down to 0 over window u_norm[j..j+n+1], estimate q̂ = (u[j+n] as u128 * BASE + u[j+n-1] as u128) / d_norm[n-1] as u128 (BASE=1u128<<64) with remainder r̂; Knuth's Test 1 (q̂==BASE, or d_norm[n-2]*q̂ > BASE*r̂+u[j+n-2]) decrements q̂/increments r̂, repeated once more if r̂<BASE — at most 2 decrements, after which q̂ equals the true digit (Theorem B). **Multiply-subtract (knuth.rs):** i128 borrow accumulator; for i in 0..=n, u[j+i] -= q̂*d_norm[i] + borrow, borrow = (product>>64) as i128 + (borrow<0) as i128; final borrow<0 triggers add-back. **Add-back (knuth.rs):** decrement q̂, add d_norm back into u[j..j+n+1] with carry that must cancel the borrow exactly (debug_assert!(carry==1)); frequency ≈ 2/BASE per digit — negligible but must be exercised by crafted fixtures. **Denormalization (knuth.rs):** collect quotient digits u_q[j] high-to-low into a capacity-q_len Vec<Limb>, strip trailing zeros for canonical form (debug_assert! no trailing zero); remainder = u_norm[0..n] right-shifted by the normalization shift (debug_assert!(remainder < *b)). Single-limb path (single.rs):

pub fn div_rem_1(a: &BigUint, b: Limb) -> Result<(BigUint, Limb), DivByZero>;

MSB-to-LSB scan with carry = (carry<<64)|limb[i], q[i] = (carry/b as u128) as Limb, carry %= b as u128; quotient normalized (trailing zeros stripped), final carry is the remainder; b==0 checked before any iteration, returning Err(DivByZero). Two-limb path (single.rs):

pub fn div_rem_2(a: &BigUint, b_hi: Limb, b_lo: Limb) -> Result<DivRem, DivByZero>;

Constructs a 128-bit divisor from b_hi,b_lo and divides each 128-bit window. Public API (lib.rs):

pub fn div_rem(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> Result<DivRem, DivByZero>;
pub fn div_rem_1(a: &BigUint, b: Limb) -> Result<(BigUint, Limb), DivByZero>;
pub fn divides(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> bool;
pub fn div_floor(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> Result<BigUint, DivByZero>;
pub fn mod_floor(a: &BigUint, b: &BigUint) -> Result<BigUint, DivByZero>;

Anchors: I5 (division identity a == q*b + r, 0 <= r < b, enforced by the add-back correction above); P8 (division has a single contract); paper P9 (Granlund & Möller, Required Reading §5 Papers) as the nlz/quotient-estimate reference underlying the normalization-shift and per-digit correction bound.

7.3 Deliverable

  • bn-div compiles; div_rem, div_rem_1, div_rem_2, divides, div_floor, mod_floor exported; CLI bn-cli calc handles / and % for arbitrary operands including multi-limb.
  • cargo test -p bn-div passes all unit + property tests; fixture suite in bn-div/tests/conformance.rs loads all 8 JSON fixture files and passes.
  • Benchmark binary bn-div/benches/knuth.rs runs without error, producing Criterion timing data at 64/256/1024/4096-bit sizes.
  • DivRem fields satisfy a = q*b + r verified in every test via an inline assertion helper; assert_canonical passes for both quotient and remainder on all fixture inputs.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
a == q*b + r for 10 000 random pairs (proptest) pass
0 <= r < b for all random pairs pass
Division by zero → Err(DivByZero), no panic pass
Single-limb path matches multi-limb for all limb-sized divisors pass

4096-bit ÷ 2048-bit throughput ≥ 5 000 ops/s on reference hardware
assert_canonical on quotient and remainder passes for all cases pass

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected
fixtures/divrem_basic.json small exact and non-exact cases, a < b, a == b exact q, r values
fixtures/divrem_single_limb.json b fits in one u64 matches single-path result
fixtures/divrem_addback.json inputs crafted to trigger add-back (Knuth example, §4.3.1) correct q, r; add-back triggered
fixtures/divrem_large.json 2048-bit ÷ 1024-bit from Python divmod match to bit
fixtures/divrem_power2.json divisors that are exact powers of 2 equivalent to shift-right
fixtures/divrem_div_by_zero.json b = 0 Err(DivByZero)
fixtures/divrem_quotient_one.json a.limbs.len() == b.limbs.len() q ∈ {0, 1}
fixtures/divrem_two_limb.json 128-bit divisor, 256-bit dividend exact match to Python

7.6 Pitfalls

  • Missing second correction: q̂ can be off by 2, not just 1; a single conditional decrement is wrong. Root cause: ignoring Knuth's two-test loop rule. Detect: fixtures/divrem_addback.json with known double-decrement case; add assert decrements <= 2.
  • Off-by-one in normalization shift: computed from wrong limb (e.g., second-highest instead of highest), producing a dividend shifted by the wrong amount. Symptom: quotient wrong in MSB digit. Debug: assert d_norm.last() >= (1<<63) immediately after normalization.
  • Remainder not denormalized: returning the shifted remainder without applying the inverse shift yields a remainder scaled by 2^shift. Caught immediately by a == q*b + r check; root cause is forgetting the final right-shift.
  • Trailing zero limbs in quotient: not stripping zeros after quotient assembly corrupts BigUint canonical form (I1). All downstream operations assume canonical form; hashing and equality silently break. Fix: call normalize_limbs (strip trailing zeros) before constructing quotient BigUint.
  • u128 overflow in q̂ computation: if the two-limb estimate is formed in u64 arithmetic before widening, it silently truncates. Always use DoubleLimb (u128) for the intermediate numerator u[j+n]*BASE + u[j+n-1].
  • Single-limb path skipped for b == 0: must check before branching to fast path; div_rem_1(a, 0) must return Err(DivByZero).
  • Quotient buffer sized incorrectly: should be exactly a.limbs.len() - b.limbs.len() + 1; over-sizing produces extra zero limbs; under-sizing is a bounds error. Pre-compute and assert.

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • Normalization copy overhead: when shift==0, special-case to an in-place reference for the normalized divisor rather than copying — saves an O(n) allocation for the common all-64-bits-used divisor case.
  • Inner multiply-subtract loop is the tightest hot path: keep as explicit limb iteration with an i128 borrow register (not abstracted through bn-add); slice the working window before the loop, and borrow d_norm as &[Limb] outside the loop to avoid repeated field-access indirection in unoptimized builds.
  • Quotient buffer: pre-allocate Vec<Limb> at exact capacity before the loop (pushing inside causes reallocation); the two-limb fast path (div_rem_2) avoids the full Algorithm D setup for the 128-bit moduli common in Barrett/Montgomery reduction (bn-mod often uses 256-bit moduli, so this still matters at the small end).

7.8 Edge Cases

  • a < b → quotient = 0, remainder = a (detected by limb-count comparison before entering the loop; no loop iterations needed).
  • a == b → quotient = 1, remainder = 0; loop runs once, q̂ = 1, subtract equals the dividend.
  • Divisor with trailing zero limbs fed in: violates canonical form; debug_assert! at entry via assert_canonical(b).
  • a.limbs.len() == b.limbs.len(): quotient is 0 or 1; the single loop iteration is correct but the q̂ test must handle n == 1 (only one divisor limb, second test not applicable).
  • a with 1 limb, b with 1 limb: route to div_rem_1.
  • Normalization shift == 63: maximum shift; dividend gains a full extra limb; ensure extra limb is initialized to 0 and included in the u_norm vector.
  • b larger than Limb::MAX but exactly 2 limbs: div_rem_2 fast path applies.

7.9 Failure Analysis

  • Silent wrong quotient: if the invariant check (a == q*b + r) is restricted to #[cfg(debug_assertions)], a Phase 8 regression that disables the Knuth path without updating tests can go undetected. Mitigation: the fixture-driven conformance tests always run in CI in both debug and release profiles.
  • Panic in add-back: if borrow handling has an off-by-one, the u64 subtraction wraps or the final carry mismatch triggers a debug_assert. Blast radius: div_rem returns Err-equivalent via panic if not caught; all callers (GCD, modular ops, rational arithmetic) fail. Recovery: structured i128 borrow accumulator prevents wrapping; the assert catches logical errors immediately.
  • Carry mismatch after add-back: the carry after adding back d_norm must be exactly 1 to cancel the borrow of -1. If carry is 0 or 2, it indicates a bug in the add-back branch condition. Detected by debug_assert!(carry == 1).
  • DivRem not satisfying contract in edge cases: a < b case must return (0, a.clone()) not (0, BigUint::zero()); a careless early return that drops the remainder is caught by the invariant check.

7.10 Operational Notes

  • Instrumentation via #[cfg(feature="trace-div")] counters (addback_count, normalization_shift_bits, quotient_digits, divisor_limbs), emittable via eprintln! or a global AtomicU64 readable by tests.
  • Benchmark suite bn-div/benches/knuth.rs sweeps 64/256/1024/4096-bit dividends with half-size divisors (Criterion-based, retained as a build artifact) and calibrates Phase 8's NEWTON_THRESHOLD.
  • Phase 8 wraps this module (div_rem_fast delegates here for small operands; Algorithm D retained as div_rem_knuth for differential testing) with no ABI break — callers of div_rem get the new dispatch automatically.
  • Each public function's rustdoc states preconditions (canonical operands, b != 0), postconditions (a == q*b + r, 0 <= r < b), and time complexity; Phase 11's bn-gcd calls div_rem_fast in the Lehmer GCD inner loop, so GCD correctness depends transitively on these invariants.

Phase 8 — Newton-Reciprocal Division + Fast Divrem

8.1 Objectives

  • Implement Newton-iteration reciprocal in bn-div/src/newton.rs: given normalized d of n limbs, compute recip = floor(B^(2n)/d) to full n-limb precision via doubling iteration, replacing the O(n^2) inner multiply-subtract loop of Algorithm D with O(M(n)) reciprocal-multiply (M(n) = Phase 5/6 multiplication cost).
  • Barrett-style precomputation DivRecip { divisor, recip } is computed once per divisor and reused for repeated divisions — key for modular arithmetic (Phase 10); enforce I5 (DivRem contract) and I6 (Newton == Knuth D for all valid inputs).
  • Threshold dispatch: sizes ≤ NEWTON_THRESHOLD limbs use Algorithm D; larger use Newton.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Module layout additions:

bn-div/
  src/
    newton.rs       — reciprocal iteration + quotient-from-reciprocal
    divrecip.rs     — DivRecip struct + repeated-division API

Reciprocal bootstrap (newton.rs): input d is normalized (MSB set); seed from the top 64 bits (d_top = d.limbs.last() as u128, r0 = (u128::MAX/d_top) as u64), with a Möller-Granlund adjustment (subtract 1) to guarantee r0*d_top <= 2^64; doubling formula r_{k+1} = 2*r_k - d*r_k^2 >> (2*working_bits) with working precision doubling each step (64→128→256→… via bn-mul::mul_fast, dispatched schoolbook/Karatsuba/Toom3 by working size) until >= n*LIMB_BITS (≈ceil(log2(n)) steps, ≈6 at 4096 bits); postcondition B^(2n)-d <= recip*d < B^(2n) checked in debug via bn-add comparison; caller must normalize d first (assert d.limbs.last() >= (1<<63)).

pub fn reciprocal(d: &BigUint) -> BigUint;  // floor(B^(2n)/d), d must be normalized
pub fn div_rem_newton(a: &BigUint, d: &BigUint, recip: &BigUint) -> DivRem;
pub fn div_rem_fast(a: &BigUint, d: &BigUint) -> Result<DivRem, DivByZero>;

Quotient from reciprocal (newton.rs): q_approx = top_bits(a*recip, 2n) (right-shift by 2n*LIMB_BITS, via bn-mul::mul_fast); r = a - q_approx*d (via bn-add::sub); correction loop while r >= d { q_approx += 1; r -= d } runs at most 2 iterations (asserted via counter); both quotient and remainder are canonicalized. Threshold dispatch (lib.rs/newton.rs): const NEWTON_THRESHOLD: usize in thresholds.rs (default 32 limbs, overridable at compile time/benchmark); div_rem_fast routes to div_rem_knuth(a,b) at or below the threshold, else Newton; div_rem_knuth is a named re-export of Phase 7's div_rem for differential testing. DivRecip (divrecip.rs):

pub struct DivRecip {
    pub divisor: BigUint,
    pub recip:   BigUint,  // floor(B^(2n)/divisor)
}
impl DivRecip {
    pub fn new(d: BigUint) -> Result<Self, DivByZero>;
    pub fn div_rem(&self, a: &BigUint) -> DivRem;
    pub fn rem(&self, a: &BigUint) -> BigUint;
}

Debug prints only limb counts of divisor/recip (never leaks secret moduli into logs); Clone (since BigUint is Clone), not Copy. Barrett integration (Phase 10, not implemented here): Barrett { modulus, mu } stores mu directly from DivRecip::recip; bn-mod depends on bn-div, never the reverse. Anchors: I6 (Newton-reciprocal divrem must equal Knuth D divrem for all valid inputs — differential-tested in 8.5); P4 (dispatch by operand size — NEWTON_THRESHOLD gates Newton vs. Knuth D exactly as MulAlgo::select gates multiply algorithms).

8.3 Deliverable

  • div_rem_fast is the canonical public entry point (delegates to Algorithm D for small operands); DivRecip exported from bn-div and used by Phase 10 Barrett without re-computation.
  • All Phase 7 conformance tests pass unchanged via div_rem_fast; Newton-vs-Knuth differential test in bn-div/tests/conformance.rs covers 5 000 random pairs, sizes 32–512 limbs, all matching.
  • Benchmark bn-div/benches/newton.rs shows Newton speedup at and above threshold.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Newton == Knuth for 5 000 random pairs, sizes 32–512 limbs (I6) pass
a == q*b + r, 0 <= r < b for Newton path (I5) pass
Correction loop ≤ 2 iterations for all test inputs assert in debug build
reciprocal(d) * d lies in [B^(2n) - d, B^(2n)) for all test inputs checked in debug
4096-bit ÷ 2048-bit speedup over Algorithm D ≥ 3× wall-clock
DivRecip repeated-division (1 000 calls, same divisor) vs fresh-reciprocal each time ≥ 2× speedup
NEWTON_THRESHOLD adjustable via thresholds.rs const without API break confirmed by recompile test

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected
fixtures/newton_vs_knuth.json 5 000 pairs, 32–512 limbs identical q, r
fixtures/newton_large.json 8192-bit ÷ 4096-bit, from Python divmod bit-exact match
fixtures/newton_correction_2.json inputs requiring exactly 2 corrections final q and r correct; correction counter == 2
fixtures/divrecip_repeated.json 100 divisions by same d using DivRecip matches fresh div_rem_fast
fixtures/newton_threshold_boundary.json inputs at NEWTON_THRESHOLD ± 1 limbs both paths produce identical result
fixtures/newton_recip_postcondition.json check recip*d range for several d lies in [B^(2n)-d, B^(2n))

8.6 Pitfalls

  • Under-iterated reciprocal: stopping one Newton step early gives an LSB error in recip; correction loop may need 3 iterations (impossible by design); the surplus manifests as a wrong quotient MSB limb. Detect: the postcondition check recip * d < B^(2n) fails. Fix: add one guard step or assert iteration count vs ceil(log2(n)).
  • Overflow in 2*recip_i step: if recip_i occupies the full working precision, doubling overflows by one bit. Fix: size working arrays to working_limbs + 1; mask or strip after each step; do not rely on BigUint auto-growth to catch it.
  • Correction loop exceeds 2 iterations: indicates multiplication (bn-mul) or the reciprocal iteration has a bug. Symptom: q_approx is off by more than 2 from the true quotient. Action: debug_assert!(corrections < 3); log a_limbs, d_limbs, recip_limbs, q_approx, r_initial to stderr and panic.
  • Aliasing in multiply: if a and recip share any underlying allocation (e.g., both derived from the same buffer), bn-mul may produce wrong results if it operates in-place. Enforce: div_rem_newton always passes distinct immutable references.
  • Threshold too low: Newton overhead (reciprocal computation + extra multiplications) costs more than Algorithm D's multiply-subtract for operands below ~2048 bits. Benchmark at each platform; update NEWTON_THRESHOLD accordingly; store the platform measurement in benches/ output.
  • DivRecip used after divisor mutation: caller conceptually modifies the effective modulus (e.g., in a loop) but reuses the old DivRecip. Encapsulate divisor; make DivRecip::divisor private; no &mut accessor.

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • Newton iteration allocates a single Vec<Limb> of size 2*n+2 before the loop, reused across doubling steps via resize (avoids O(log n) separate allocations); the full a*recip product is 4n limbs but only the top n are needed — a future mul_high(a, b, n_out_limbs) variant in bn-mul is noted as an optimization target, not required in Phase 5.
  • Correction multiplication q_approx*d reuses bn-mul; for small corrections consider bn-add's add-mul-1 primitive instead of a full multiply.
  • Cache: recip for a 4096-bit divisor is 512 B (fits L1); for 32 768-bit operands it's 4 KB (L1 eviction likely — measure via perf stat); re-computing DivRecip::new(d) inside loops is a common mistake — document Arc<DivRecip> for shared ownership in module-level rustdoc.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • d has 1 limb: reciprocal degenerates; route to div_rem_1 (single-limb path) to avoid Newton overhead.
  • a < d: quotient = 0, remainder = a; detect before computing reciprocal (check limb counts and MSB).
  • d is an exact power of 2: reciprocal is exact; correction runs 0 iterations; assert in fixture.
  • a.limbs.len() == 2 * d.limbs.len() (tight-fit): the (a * recip) >> shift index lands exactly at limb boundary; verify off-by-one in shift index.
  • Operands at exactly NEWTON_THRESHOLD: both code paths produce identical results (verified by fixtures/newton_threshold_boundary.json).
  • Very large operands (256 limbs = 16 384-bit): reciprocal takes ≈ 8 iterations; timing should remain under 10 ms on reference hardware.
  • DivRecip::new called with magnitude-zero BigUint: returns Err(DivByZero) before any computation.

8.9 Failure Analysis

  • Wrong quotient, no assertion fired: if the I5 invariant check is debug-only and Newton has a subtraction bug, production callers of bn-mod or bn-gcd compute incorrect results silently. Mitigation: retain a full-invariant integration test that runs in release profile in CI; instrument via feature flag.
  • Infinite correction loop: if reciprocal precision is off by more than 1 ULP, correction never terminates. Guard: while corrections < 3 { ... } with explicit panic on third iteration; emit operand sizes and intermediate values as diagnostic context.
  • DivRecip with zero divisor reaches reciprocal: if DivRecip::new fails to check d.is_zero() early, reciprocal encounters a zero top limb and produces an undefined result (division by zero in seed step). Fix: gate at DivRecip::new entry with an explicit is_zero check.
  • Phase 10 Barrett using stale DivRecip: if the modulus changes after Barrett is constructed, reduction silently uses the wrong reciprocal. Fix: Barrett stores DivRecip by value; treat Barrett as immutable after construction.

8.10 Operational Notes

  • Tracing feature trace-newton: per-call log line with a_limbs, d_limbs, recip_limbs, corrections, newton_iterations, algo (Newton|Knuth), written to stderr or a tracing subscriber.
  • Benchmark bn-div/benches/newton.rs sweeps divisor sizes 1–256 limbs, outputs a CSV of ops/s for Newton and Knuth per size (CI preserves it as a build artifact; regressions detected via median comparison across commits).
  • DivRecip implements Debug (limb counts only), Clone, not Copy; Phase 10 Barrett imports it directly (mu = the recip field, Barrett::new(m) builds DivRecip::new(m) internally, no redundant reciprocal).
  • Compatibility: div_rem_fast as canonical entry point is additive (div_rem_knuth remains for differential testing; Phase 7's div_rem becomes an alias needing no caller updates); Phase 10 Montgomery uses DivRecip for R mod m/R^2 mod m setup, and Phase 11 modpow depends transitively on Phase 8 — validated end-to-end by Phase 14's conformance suite.

Phase 9 — Signed BigInt

9.1 Objectives

  • Implement BigInt { sign: Sign, mag: BigUint } in bn-int (Sign from bn-core); invariant I2 (enforced by construction): sign == NoSign iff mag.is_zero(), never Plus/Minus on a zero magnitude.
  • Arithmetic (Add, Sub, Mul, Div, Rem) via sign-aware dispatch to bn-add/bn-uint/bn-mul/bn-div magnitude ops; bitwise (BitAnd, BitOr, BitXor, Not) uses two's-complement infinite-precision semantics with no actual two's-complement storage.
  • Shifts: Shl<u64>, Shr<u64> (arithmetic; rounds toward −∞ for negative); utilities: abs, signum, negate, is_zero, pow, Ord, Eq, Hash, Display, From<i64>, TryFrom<BigInt> for i64.
  • Division semantics: default Div/Rem are truncated (toward zero); div_floor/mod_floor round toward −∞; both documented explicitly.

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Module layout:

bn-int/
  src/
    lib.rs          — BigInt struct, constructors, Ord/Eq/Hash, From/TryFrom, Display
    arith.rs        — Add, Sub, Mul, Div, Rem, pow
    bitwise.rs      — BitAnd, BitOr, BitXor, Not, Shl, Shr
    convert.rs      — From<i64>, TryFrom<BigInt> for i64, From<BigUint>
  tests/            — arith.rs (signed arithmetic), bitwise.rs (two's-complement), conformance.rs (fixture-driven)

Construction (lib.rs):

pub fn from_magnitude(sign: Sign, mag: BigUint) -> BigInt;
// Enforces I2: if mag.is_zero() { sign = NoSign }
pub fn one() -> BigInt; pub fn minus_one() -> BigInt; pub fn zero() -> BigInt;
pub fn is_zero(&self) -> bool; pub fn is_positive(&self) -> bool /* sign==Plus */; pub fn is_negative(&self) -> bool /* sign==Minus */;
pub fn into_parts(self) -> (Sign, BigUint);  // destructure; I2 holds on output

BigInt fields (sign, mag) are private, so all construction goes through named constructors or from_magnitude, enforcing I2 at the type level without unsafe. Sign-dispatch table for addition (arith.rs):

self.sign rhs.sign Operation Result sign
Plus Plus mag_add(self.mag, rhs.mag) Plus (unless zero)
Minus Minus mag_add Minus (unless zero)
Plus Minus compare mags; mag_sub(larger, smaller) sign of larger; NoSign if equal
Minus Plus compare mags; mag_sub(larger, smaller) sign of larger; NoSign if equal
Any NoSign clone self self.sign
NoSign Any clone rhs rhs.sign
All branches call from_magnitude at return to enforce I2.
Subtraction (arith.rs): a - b = a + (-b) via negate (no separate dispatch code); the consuming Sub<BigInt> for BigInt uses into_negate(rhs) to avoid a clone, while Sub<&BigInt> for &BigInt clones and negates (matches ergonomic Rust patterns).
Multiplication (arith.rs): magnitude via bn-mul::mul_fast(&self.mag, &rhs.mag); sign = Plus if both operands share a non-zero sign, Minus if opposite, NoSign if either is zero (magnitude product skipped when either operand is zero).
Division (arith.rs): truncated Div/Rem divide magnitudes via bn-div::div_rem_fast (quotient sign = product-of-signs, remainder sign = dividend sign, matching Rust i64); sign-combination table:
a sign b sign q sign r sign
--------- --------- --------- ---------
Plus Plus Plus Plus or NoSign
Plus Minus Minus Plus or NoSign
Minus Plus Minus Minus or NoSign
Minus Minus Plus Minus or NoSign
div_floor/mod_floor adjust when remainder≠0 and signs differ (q -= 1, r += divisor; remainder sign = divisor sign, matching Python ///%), documented in rustdoc following num-integer naming; division by zero panics in BigInt::div (consistent with Rust primitives), with checked_div returning Option<BigInt>.
Signed comparison (lib.rs): total order Minus < NoSign < Plus; within Plus compare mag ascending (BigUint::cmp), within Minus compare mag descending (larger magnitude is more negative); PartialOrd derives from Ord, Eq consistent with Ord/Hash; Hash hashes sign and mag together (canonical form I1/I2 guarantees equal values hash equally, and zero's unique representation — NoSign, empty mag — makes BigInt::zero().hash(...) consistent regardless of construction path).
Two's-complement bitwise ops (bitwise.rs): for x >= 0 the two's-complement representation is the magnitude; for x < 0 it is NOT(mag(x)-1) with infinite sign extension (bit i of x is the inverse of bit i of -x-1); identities used (no infinite-width storage needed): NOT x = -(x+1); (-a) AND b = NOT(a-1) AND b; (-a) AND (-b) = -(((a-1) OR (b-1))+1); (-a) OR b = -(((a-1) AND NOT(b))+1); (-a) OR (-b) = -(((a-1) AND (b-1))+1); (-a) XOR b = -(((a-1) XOR b)+1); (-a) XOR (-b) = ((a-1) XOR (b-1)). Implementation: NOT x = -(x+1) is simply x.add(BigInt::one()).negate(); AND/OR/XOR decompose by sign case via the identities above, with sub-operations on BigUint magnitudes via bn-add/bn-uint bitwise primitives; a bitwise.rs helper apply_binop(op: BitwiseOp, a: &BigInt, b: &BigInt) -> BigInt dispatches to the correct identity by sign combination, sizing any temporary buffers to max(self.mag.bit_len(), rhs.mag.bit_len()) + 1 bits.
Arithmetic shifts (bitwise.rs): Shl<u64> delegates to bn-uint shift-left on the magnitude (sign unchanged, result stays canonical); Shr<u64> for Plus/NoSign delegates to bn-uint shift-right (zero-fill from MSB, NoSign if the result is zero); Shr<u64> for Minus shifts the magnitude right and adds 1 if any shifted-out bit was 1 (trailing_bits_nonzero), ensuring floor semantics ((-1)>>n == -1, (-2)>>1 == -1, (-3)>>1 == -2); shift by 0 is identity (no allocation), and shift amount ≥ bit_len yields 0 for Plus and -1 for Minus.
Key signatures: 
impl BigInt {
    pub fn abs(&self) -> BigUint;
    pub fn signum(&self) -> i32;         // -1 | 0 | 1
    pub fn negate(&self) -> BigInt;
    pub fn into_negate(self) -> BigInt;  // consuming variant; avoids clone
    pub fn pow(&self, exp: u64) -> BigInt;
    pub fn checked_div(&self, rhs: &BigInt) -> Option<BigInt>;
    pub fn div_floor(&self, rhs: &BigInt) -> BigInt;
    pub fn mod_floor(&self, rhs: &BigInt) -> BigInt;
    pub fn bit_len(&self) -> u64;
    pub fn gcd(&self, other: &BigInt) -> BigUint;  // delegates to bn-gcd on magnitudes
}

Standard operator trait impls, all for BigInt with Output = BigInt: Add, Sub, Mul, Div, Rem, BitAnd, BitOr, BitXor, Not, Shl<u64>, Shr<u64>. Plus Ord; Display (matches Python str(int): leading - for negative); From<i64>; TryFrom<BigInt> for i64; From<BigUint> for BigInt (sign = Plus unless zero); From<BigInt> for BigUint (panics if negative — use TryFrom for the safe variant).

9.3 Deliverable

  • bn-int crate compiles with all trait impls listed above present; bn-cli calc handles negative integer literals, unary minus, and signed +, -, *, /, %, **, ~, &, |, ^, >>, <<.
  • cargo test -p bn-int passes all unit, property, and conformance tests; BigInt::to_string() (via Display) agrees with Python str(int) for all fixture values; From<i64> / TryFrom<BigInt> for i64 roundtrip passes for all 2^17 sampled i64 values.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
a + (-a) == 0 for 5 000 random BigInt values (I2 enforced) pass
a * b sign rule correct for all 9 sign-combination entries 4-case + zero cases, pass
NOT x == -(x+1) for 2 000 random signed ints (positive + negative) pass
Arithmetic right shift: (-5) >> 1 == -3 and (-1) >> 100 == -1 pass
signum returns {-1, 0, 1} for each sign class pass
from_magnitude(_, zero_mag) always yields sign == NoSign pass
Roundtrip From<i64> + TryFrom<BigInt> for all i64 sample pass
div_floor vs truncated div agree for same-sign operands; differ for mixed-sign remainder pass

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected
fixtures/bigint_arith.json add/sub/mul/div/rem for all sign combos including zero operands matches Python int
fixtures/bigint_bitwise.json AND/OR/XOR/NOT for positive, negative, zero, mixed matches Python int bitwise
fixtures/bigint_shift.json left/right shift; negative right shift; shift by 0; shift exceeding bit_len matches Python int >> n
fixtures/bigint_divrem_sign.json truncated vs floor div for all four sign combos with nonzero remainder signed semantics table
fixtures/bigint_display.json Display output for positive, negative, zero, large values exact string match
fixtures/bigint_compare.json ordering across all sign classes and equal magnitudes total order; transitivity
fixtures/bigint_from_i64.json From<i64> for boundaries: i64::MIN, i64::MAX, -1, 0, 1 exact BigInt values

9.6 Pitfalls

  • Sign not coerced to NoSign on zero result: if mag_sub(a, a) returns zero magnitude but sign is left as Plus, I2 is violated; equality checks and hashing silently break. Fix: always route through from_magnitude at every arithmetic return point. Wrong branch for equal magnitudes in opposite-sign sub: a + (-a) with internal path reaching mag_sub(equal, equal) must produce NoSign; an off-by-one in the magnitude comparison sends it to Plus or Minus. Detect: property test a + (-a) == 0 for large values.
  • NOT implemented as raw limb bitwise NOT: ~BigInt is NOT a limb-level flip; it must equal -(x+1). Raw limb inversion produces wrong results for positive values (no sign extension) and panics on negative (magnitude underflow). Test: ~0 == -1, ~(-1) == 0. Arithmetic right shift without floor rounding: (-5) >> 1 must yield -3 (floor), not -2 (truncation). Root cause: reusing bn-uint shift directly without adding 1 for the fractional bit. Assert in fixture bigint_shift.json.
  • Truncated vs floor division confusion: if mod_floor delegates to the truncated rem for positive results but the remainder is negative, callers in bn-convert and bn-gcd may receive a negative modulus. Fix: document both, test all four sign combinations. Allocation on every negate: negate(&self) clones the magnitude; callers in a tight loop see excessive allocation. Provide into_negate(self) -> BigInt (consuming, in-place sign flip, no clone) and use it in Sub operator overload.
  • From<BigInt> for BigUint panic: calling this on a negative BigInt panics; this is intentional but must be documented. Callers in bn-gcd::gcd should use .abs() explicitly rather than From.

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • Sign-dispatch branch prediction: the sign check before magnitude add/sub is a shallow two-level branch; GCD's alternating-sign call pattern is fairly predictable and needs no special mitigation. Two's-complement bitwise temporaries: to_twos_unsigned allocates a limb vector of width max_bits+1 (33 limbs for a 2048-bit negative operand — acceptable); profile bitwise-heavy workloads with cargo flamegraph.
  • Magnitude comparison before subtraction is O(n), adding one scan before the O(n) sub (2n limb ops total — unavoidable in sign-magnitude representation). BigInt cloning in operator overloads: provide both Add<&BigInt> and Add<BigInt> impls so the consuming form avoids a clone when the right operand is already owned; pow(exp: u64) uses naive repeated squaring via bn-mul and is for small exponents only — Phase 11 modpow is the right tool at cryptographic scale.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • BigInt::zero() operations: 0 + x == x, 0 * x == 0, x / 0 panics (document this), x % 0 panics. i64::MIN as BigInt: magnitude = 2^63, sign = Minus; TryFrom<BigInt> for i64 must handle this correctly (value fits; -2^63 is valid i64).
  • i64::MIN.into_negate(): magnitude = 2^63, sign = Plus; value 2^63 does not fit in i64; TryFrom must return Err. Bitwise on zero: 0 & x == 0, 0 | x == x, 0 ^ x == x, ~0 == -1.
  • Right shift of Plus by amount ≥ bit_len: result = 0. Right shift of Minus by any amount: result ≥ -1; (-1) >> n == -1 for all n ≥ 0.
  • Left shift of negative: sign preserved; magnitude grows; (-3) << 2 == -12. Multiply Minus * Minus where both magnitudes are nonzero: result is Plus; magnitude product from bn-mul is nonzero; ensure from_magnitude(Plus, nonzero_mag) leaves sign as Plus.
  • pow(0) = 1 for any base including 0^0 (defined as 1 per mathematical convention used here; document explicitly).

9.9 Failure Analysis

  • Silent wrong sign: if from_magnitude is bypassed via direct struct construction (possible within the same crate module), I2 violations propagate. Mitigation: make BigInt fields private; all construction via from_magnitude or named constructors; enforce in code review. Bitwise NOT regression: a change to the helper that drops the + 1 step produces -(x) instead of -(x+1) for positive integers; all NOT tests in fixtures/bigint_bitwise.json fail immediately. No blast-radius concern; the failure is deterministic and immediate.
  • Division sign bug corrupting GCD: if BigInt::rem returns wrong sign for negative operands, Lehmer GCD (Phase 11) receives a negative remainder, enters wrong branch, and either returns wrong GCD or loops. Root cause: this phase's mod_floor semantics. Mitigation: bn-gcd operates on BigUint magnitudes only, using BigInt::abs() before dispatch; sign errors in BigInt::rem do not reach GCD if the interface is designed correctly. TryFrom<BigInt> for i64 overflow: if the conversion does not check magnitude against i64::MAX / i64::MIN range, it silently truncates. Implement by comparing magnitude limb count (> 1 means out of range) and single-limb value against bounds.

9.10 Operational Notes

  • No runtime metrics for this crate — pure in-memory computation, no I/O or syscalls; Debug prints sign and mag.limbs.len() only (avoids logging secrets or multi-megabyte integers by accident). CI matrix runs cargo test -p bn-int with RUSTFLAGS="-C overflow-checks=on" to catch silently-saturating limb arithmetic, and under Miri for memory safety of any unsafe blocks introduced for performance.
  • Consumed by Phase 11 (GCD), Phase 12 (base conversion with sign), and Phase 13 (public API/rationals); the sign rules established here must not change without updating all three downstream crates. From<BigUint> for BigInt is a zero-cost conversion (moves the Vec<Limb>, sign = Plus unless zero); Display here uses temporary iterative small-divmod, replaced in Phase 12 by an API-compatible divide-and-conquer base converter.
  • A module-level comment in bn-int/src/arith.rs documents the complete signed division convention (truncated by default, floor via explicit method) to prevent ambiguity when Phase 13 adds BigRational::floor; proptest strategies generate BigInt values across all size classes and all three signs for the arith/bitwise/shift property suites.

Phase 10 — Modular Arithmetic: Barrett + Montgomery

10.1 Objectives

  • Implement Barrett reduction in bn-mod: precompute mu = floor(b^(2k) / m), reduce via two multiplications + corrections.
  • Implement Montgomery form in bn-mod: REDC algorithm, in/out transforms, modular multiply without trial division.
  • Implement modular add/sub/mul keeping results in [0, m).
  • Expose Barrett and Montgomery context structs (locked types); callers choose per use case.
  • Constraint: no floating-point; all arithmetic on BigUint limbs via bn-mul/bn-div primitives.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

bn-mod/src/barrett.rs

  • Barrett::new(m: &BigUint) -> Barrett: compute k = m.limb_len(), b^(2k) as a 1-bit shifted BigUint, mu = b^(2k) / m using bn-div Knuth D; store modulus, mu, k.
  • barrett_reduce(x: &BigUint, ctx: &Barrett) -> BigUint: steps:
    1. q1 = x >> (k-1) limbs (right-shift by (k-1)*LIMB_BITS).
    2. q2 = q1 * mu via bn-mul dispatch.
    3. q3 = q2 >> (k+1) limbs.
    4. r1 = x mod b^(k+1) (low k+1 limbs).
    5. r2 = (q3 * m) mod b^(k+1).
    6. r = r1 - r2; if negative add b^(k+1).
    7. At most 2 subtractions of m to bring r < m.
  • barrett_mul_mod(a, b, ctx) -> BigUint: multiply a*b via bn-mul, then barrett_reduce.

bn-mod/src/montgomery.rs

  • Montgomery::new(m: &BigUint) -> Montgomery: m must be odd; R = b^k where k = m.limb_len(); compute n_prime (Montgomery constant: -m^{-1} mod b per limb, one-limb inverse via extended GCD on low limb); compute r2 = R^2 mod m via Barrett or repeated squaring.
  • Montgomery::into_form(x: &BigUint) -> BigUint: mont_mul(x, r2, ctx) — transforms x -> xR mod m.
  • Montgomery::from_form(t: &BigUint) -> BigUint: redc(t) — transforms tR^{-1} mod m.
  • redc(t: &BigUint, ctx: &Montgomery) -> BigUint: for each limb i: u = (t[i] * n_prime) mod b; add u * m * b^i to t; divide by b^k; conditional subtract m.
  • mont_mul(a, b, ctx) -> BigUint: redc(a * b) (multiply via bn-mul, then REDC).

bn-mod/src/lib.rs

  • mod_add(a, b, m) -> BigUint: add, subtract m if >= m.
  • mod_sub(a, b, m) -> BigUint: subtract, add m if negative (borrow detected).
  • mod_mul(a, b, m, method: ReductionMethod) -> BigUint: dispatch to Barrett or Montgomery.
  • ReductionMethod enum: { Barrett, Montgomery, Auto }.

Anchors: I7 (every modular result in [0, m); Montgomery roundtrip from_mont(to_mont(x)) == x mod m); I12 (Montgomery round-trip / domain non-leakage — into_form/from_form must bracket every public bn-mod entry point so no caller ever observes an xR mod m value); P6 (Montgomery/Barrett contexts are precomputed once via new() and reused, never rebuilt per operation).

10.3 Deliverable

  • bn-mod crate compiles; Barrett::new, Montgomery::new, barrett_reduce, redc, mont_mul, mod_add, mod_sub, mod_mul all callable.
  • Unit tests pass: barrett_reduce(a*b, ctx) == (a*b) % m for 100 random m,a,b.
  • Montgomery roundtrip: from_form(into_form(x)) == x mod m for 50 random odd m.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Barrett correctness barrett_reduce(x, ctx) == x % m for x up to 2*m^2, 1000 random cases
Montgomery roundtrip from_form(into_form(x)) == x mod m, 500 odd moduli
mod_mul vs naive (a * b) % m == mod_mul(a,b,m,Auto) for 1000 random pairs
Modulus m=1 result always 0
m is power-of-2 Barrett still correct (NTT primes excluded)
No trailing zero limbs I1 maintained after every reduction

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose
fixtures/barrett_reduce.json {m, x, expected} tuples; covers x < m, m <= x < 2m, x up to 10*m
fixtures/montgomery_mul.json {m_odd, a, b, expected} (a*b mod m); m up to 256 limbs
fixtures/mod_arith_roundtrip.json add/sub/mul chains; final vs Python pow(a,e,m) reference
fixtures/barrett_boundary.json x = b^(2k) - 1, m = b^k - 1; overflow boundary
fixtures/malformed_mod.json m=0 → DivByZero; even m to Montgomery → InvalidModulus error

10.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal
Off-by-one in k selection barrett_reduce produces values >= m k = limb_len vs k = bit_len / LIMB_BITS mismatch assertion r < m fails sporadically
Montgomery odd-modulus skip wrong result for even m n_prime undefined for even panic in inverse; add guard in new()
REDC limb-count mismatch garbled output t has 2k limbs but loop runs k inject size assertion before REDC loop
Barrett double-subtract omission result in [m, 2m) correction loop exits early add post-condition assert r < m in debug mode
r2 computed before m stored Montgomery modular pow uses stale r2 out-of-order init constructor ordering; reviewed by inspection
Carry loss in REDC accumulate subtle overflow by 1 intermediate t buffer too short extend t by one extra limb for carry

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Barrett::new is O(n log n) per construction — cache context across repeated reduces (e.g., modpow inner loop).
  • Montgomery REDC: inner loop iterates k times, each iteration a single-limb multiply-add; keep m and t in L1 cache by chunking for large k.
  • mont_mul calls bn-mul dispatch (Phase 5 Karatsuba/Toom for large k); avoid re-allocating the product buffer per call — pass a scratch buffer.
  • mod_mul with Auto: choose Montgomery for odd m and repeated use (context amortization), Barrett otherwise.
  • Avoid BigUint clone inside reduction; operate on borrowed LimbsMut where possible.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • m = 1 → all residues are 0; ensure barrett_reduce doesn't return 1.
  • a = 0 or b = 0 in mod_mul → 0 without entering reduction.
  • x >= b^(2k) input to Barrett → undefined; callers must guarantee x < b^(2k); add debug assert.
  • m with leading limb = 0 (non-canonical) → I1 violated; Barrett::new must reject or normalize.
  • Very small m (single limb) → fast path via u128 remainder without full limb-vector arithmetic.

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Barrett over-reduces property test r < m fails wrong modpow output recheck k, correction loop count
REDC infinite loop loop-invariant assert hang in modpow bound loop to k+2; panic on exceeded
n_prime wrong Montgomery mul test fails all Montgomery results wrong cross-check n_prime via m * n_prime ≡ -1 mod b
Alloc pressure in inner loop bench shows GC spikes latency in modpow preallocate scratch Vec outside loop

10.10 Operational Notes

  • Expose #[cfg(feature = "trace-reduction")] to log (k, method, operand_limbs) per call for algorithm analysis.
  • Benchmark fixture: cargo bench --bench mod_bench for barrett_reduce and mont_mul across limb counts 1, 4, 16, 64, 256.
  • Montgomery::new panics on even modulus in debug; returns Err(InvalidModulus) in release.
  • Context structs are Clone + Debug; not Send unless limb vectors are Send (they are for Vec<u64>).

Phase 11 — Modular Exponentiation + GCD + Inverse

11.1 Objectives

  • Implement windowed/sliding-window modular exponentiation in bn-mod over Montgomery form; offer constant-time path (P9, I7).
  • Implement binary (Stein) GCD and Lehmer GCD in bn-gcd.
  • Implement extended GCD; derive modular inverse via ext-gcd and Fermat's little theorem (prime modulus path).
  • Constraint: constant-time modpow must avoid data-dependent branching on secret exponent bits.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

bn-mod/src/modpow.rs

  • modpow(base, exp, m) -> BigUint: dispatcher; chooses Montgomery for odd m, Barrett otherwise.
  • modpow_montgomery(base, exp, m, window_size: u8) -> BigUint:
    1. Convert base to Montgomery form: base_m = into_form(base).
    2. Precompute table T[i] = base_m^i mod m for i in 0..2^window_size.
    3. Scan exp bits right-to-left in windows of window_size; accumulate via mont_mul.
    4. Convert result from_form.
  • modpow_ct(base, exp, m) -> BigUint: constant-time variant; use Montgomery ladder or always-multiply-always-square with cmov on table index; document that branch-free behavior depends on no short-circuit in mont_mul.
  • Window size selection: w=4 for exp >= 512 bits, w=2 for 128–511 bits, w=1 (square-and-multiply) below 128 bits.

bn-gcd/src/binary.rs

  • gcd_binary(a: BigUint, b: BigUint) -> BigUint: Stein's algorithm; factor out common powers of 2 via trailing_zeros (Phase 3); halving loop; result is gcd * 2^k.
  • Terminates in O(n^2) bit operations; correct for gcd(0,x) = x, gcd(0,0) = 0.

bn-gcd/src/lehmer.rs

  • gcd_lehmer(a: BigUint, b: BigUint) -> BigUint: extract top two limbs as a u64 approximation; compute Lehmer matrix [[A,B],[C,D]] via single-precision loop; apply matrix to (a,b) pair to advance by ~LIMB_BITS bits per Lehmer step; fall back to one Euclidean step when b has fewer than 2 limbs; recurse.
  • Lehmer reduces bit length by ~64 bits per matrix application vs ~1 bit per Stein step.

bn-gcd/src/extended.rs

  • extended_gcd(a: &BigInt, b: &BigInt) -> (BigInt, BigInt, BigInt): returns (g, x, y) with a*x + b*y = g; iterative using Bezout coefficient tracking with signed BigInt (Phase 9).
  • mod_inverse(a: &BigUint, m: &BigUint) -> Result<BigUint, DivByZero>: ext-gcd; if gcd != 1 return Err(NoInverse); normalize Bezout coefficient to [0, m).
  • mod_inverse_fermat(a: &BigUint, p: &BigUint) -> BigUint: modpow(a, p-2, p); caller asserts p is prime; no GCD required.

bn-gcd/src/lib.rs

  • gcd(a, b) -> BigUint: dispatch to Lehmer for >= 4 limbs, binary otherwise.
  • lcm(a, b) -> BigUint: a / gcd(a,b) * b to avoid overflow.

Anchors: P9 (constant-time — modpow_ct's branchless table select); paper P6 (Lehmer, Required Reading §5 Papers) as the direct basis for gcd_lehmer's single-precision matrix approximation; papers P11/P12 (Bernstein; Bernstein & Yang, Required Reading §5 Papers) for the cofactor-batching and constant-time divstep techniques extended_gcd/mod_inverse build on.

11.3 Deliverable

  • bn-mod/modpow, bn-gcd crates compile; modpow, gcd, extended_gcd, mod_inverse, mod_inverse_fermat all callable.
  • modpow(2, 100, 1000000007) matches Python reference.
  • gcd(0, x) == x, gcd(x, 0) == x, gcd(a, b) == gcd(b, a) for random 200-limb inputs.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
modpow vs Python pow(b,e,m) 1000 random (b,e,m) tuples, all size classes
gcd vs gcd_binary cross-check 500 pairs, Lehmer == binary
extended_gcd Bezout identity a*x + b*y == g for 500 pairs
mod_inverse correctness a * mod_inverse(a,m) % m == 1 for 500 coprime pairs
Constant-time path no secret-dependent branching; validated by code inspection + cargo-careful
GCD invariants gcd(0,0) == 0; gcd(1,x) == 1; gcd(p,p) == p

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose
fixtures/modpow_vectors.json {base, exp, m, expected}; exp=0,1,2,large; m=prime,composite,power-of-2
fixtures/gcd_vectors.json {a, b, expected_gcd}; includes a=0, b=0, coprime, equal, power-of-2

11.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug
Lehmer matrix sign error GCD wrong for ~1 in 10^4 inputs matrix coefficient off-by-one at single-precision boundary diff-test against binary GCD on random inputs
Modpow table not in Montgomery form wrong result on 2nd+ squaring forgot to transform precomputed T[i] assert T[0] == into_form(1)
Ext-GCD Bezout negative result modular inverse negative signed Bezout coefficient not normalized to [0,m) add while x < 0 { x += m } post-step
Window precomputation odd sizes off-by-one in table index 2^w vs 2^w - 1 table entries test w=1,2,4 separately
gcd(0,0) returns junk incorrect for degenerate case missing early return explicit if a.is_zero() && b.is_zero() return zero
Constant-time cmov elided by compiler data-dependent timing optimizer collapses if ct_bool branch use subtle::Choice or intrinsic; audit with cargo-asm

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • Modpow: dominant cost is (exp.bit_len()) Montgomery multiplications; table precomputation cost amortized over 2^w - 1 entries vs window size benefit; optimal w per exponent size (table above).
  • Lehmer: reduces bit length ~64 bits/step vs Stein's ~1 bit/step; for 2048-bit inputs, ~32 Lehmer steps vs ~2048 Stein halving iterations.
  • Precompute Montgomery table outside the exponent loop; do not re-enter Montgomery form per bit.
  • lcm intermediate a/gcd before multiply prevents operand size explosion.
  • Avoid BigUint cloning in GCD recursion; swap references where possible.

11.8 Edge Cases

  • exp = 0 → modpow returns 1 (even if base = 0, per convention).
  • base = 0, exp > 0 → 0.
  • m = 1 → modpow returns 0.
  • mod_inverse(a, m) where a >= m → reduce first.
  • extended_gcd(0, b) → (b, 0, 1).
  • Even modulus in modpow_montgomery → must fall back to Barrett path.
  • Lehmer fallback when b drops to 1 limb mid-iteration; must not apply matrix then.

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Modpow silent wrong output mismatch vs Python reference all cryptographic consumers broken rerun fixtures/modpow_vectors.json; bisect exponent size
Lehmer divergence GCD != binary GCD on fuzz incorrect rational reductions in Phase 13 disable Lehmer, use binary only; file bug
Constant-time bypass timing side-channel cryptographic key exposure audit cargo-asm; switch to subtle crate patterns
mod_inverse panic on m=0 DivByZero propagated upstream call site crash m=0 returns Err(DivByZero) before GCD

11.10 Operational Notes

  • BIGNUM_TRACE_MODPOW=1 env flag (debug builds): log (exp_bits, window_size, mont_muls_count) per call.
  • Constant-time path gated by cfg(feature = "ct-modpow"); default OFF; consumers opt in.
  • Benchmark: cargo bench --bench gcd_bench and modpow_bench across bit sizes 64, 256, 1024, 4096.
  • gcd_lehmer vs gcd_binary toggle via feature flag for differential testing during CI.

Phase 12 — Base Conversion + Parse/Format

12.1 Objectives

  • Implement bn-convert: to/from bases 2, 8, 10, 16, and arbitrary base N.
  • Schoolbook path (repeated divmod by base^chunk) for small values (<= threshold limbs).
  • Divide-and-conquer path for large values: recursive split by a precomputed power of the base, halving operand size each level.
  • Parse with input validation; format with optional grouping separators.
  • Invariant: I9 (roundtrip exactness) enforced by property tests.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

bn-convert/src/schoolbook.rs

  • to_base_schoolbook(x: &BigUint, base: u32) -> Vec<u32>: repeatedly divide x by base (using bn-div single-limb fast path); collect remainders as digits in reverse; O(n^2) where n = output digit count.
  • chunk_base(base: u32, chunk_size: usize) -> BigUint: base^chunk_size as BigUint; cached per (base, chunk_size).
  • Schoolbook threshold: use when x.limb_len() <= CONVERT_SCHOOLBOOK_THRESHOLD (default 32 limbs).

bn-convert/src/divconq.rs

  • to_base_divconq(x: &BigUint, base: u32, pow_table: &[BigUint]) -> Vec<u32>:
    1. Build pow_table[i] = base^(2^i) up to >= x; memoize across calls for the same base.
    2. Split: (hi, lo) = divrem(x, pow_table[k]) where pow_table[k] ~ sqrt(x).
    3. Recurse on hi and lo; concatenate digit strings with zero-padding lo to exact width.
    4. Base case: schoolbook when x.limb_len() <= threshold.
  • Complexity: O(M(n) log n) where M(n) is multiplication cost at n-limb size.

bn-convert/src/parse.rs

  • from_str_base(s: &str, base: u32) -> Result<BigUint, ParseError>:
    1. Validate: base in 2..=36; each char in valid alphabet; reject empty string.
    2. Schoolbook accumulate: acc = acc * base + digit_val; uses bn-mul add-mul-1 primitive.
    3. For large inputs (>= CONVERT_PARSE_THRESHOLD chars): split string at midpoint, recurse, combine hi * base^half_len + lo.
    4. Strip leading zeros; normalize (I1).
  • from_str_radix_prefix(s: &str) -> Result<(BigUint, u32), ParseError>: auto-detect 0x, 0b, 0o prefixes.

bn-convert/src/format.rs

  • to_string_base(x: &BigUint, base: u32) -> String: call to_base_divconq or schoolbook per threshold; assemble digit chars; prepend 0 if empty (for zero).
  • to_string_grouped(x: &BigUint, base: u32, group_size: usize, sep: char) -> String: insert sep every group_size digits from the right.
  • Display for BigUint: calls to_string_base(x, 10).
  • LowerHex/UpperHex for BigUint: calls to_string_base(x, 16) with case mapping.

bn-convert/src/lib.rs

  • Base enum dispatching: Base10 -> base=10, Base16 -> base=16, BaseN(u32) -> base=u32.
  • CONVERT_SCHOOLBOOK_THRESHOLD: tunable constant, default 32 limbs (empirically measured crossover).
  • Re-export parse, format functions; implement FromStr for BigUint, BigInt, BigRational.

12.3 Deliverable

  • bn-convert crate compiles; from_str_base, to_string_base, from_str_radix_prefix callable.
  • parse(format(x, 10), 10) == x for all test vectors; same for base 16.
  • BigUint::from_str("1234567890123456789012345678901234567890") parses correctly vs Python int(...).
  • to_string_base(factorial(100), 10) matches known value.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Roundtrip parse(format(x, b), b) == x 1000 random x, bases 2/8/10/16/36
Divide-and-conquer == schoolbook 500 inputs spanning schoolbook and D&C thresholds
Large decimal: 10000-limb number to_string_base completes; D&C engaged
Parse invalid input ParseError for empty, bad char, base out of range
from_str("0") == BigUint::zero() I1: no trailing zero limbs
Grouping format digits correct; separator at correct positions

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose
fixtures/parse_decimal.json {input_str, expected_hex} for known values (factorials, Fibonacci, powers of 10)
fixtures/roundtrip_bases.json {hex_magnitude, bases: [2,8,10,16,36]}; roundtrip each base
fixtures/parse_errors.json {input, base, error_kind} for invalid strings
fixtures/large_decimal.json 1000-digit decimal strings; parse + roundtrip
